<div class="issue-lead">
<h2 class="issue-category-label">01 &middot; Leadership</h2>
<h1 class="issue-story-heading issue-lead-headline">The <em class="issue-emphasis">Antithesis</em> Principle</h1>
<div class="issue-lead-body">Any truth about human nature can be pointed two ways: outward as a tactic for working people, or inward as a warning about your own defaults. Smart people chase the tactic, wise people eliminate the default in themselves first: &quot;You will not only reach the obvious conclusion that everyone who&#39;s smart reaches, but perhaps also the non-obvious one that only the wise do.&quot;</div>
<div class="issue-lead-cta">

<div class="issue-story">
<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="/2026/08/09/stories/why-do-i-write-f9951f/">Why Do I Write?</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="why-do-i-write-f9951f"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">Writing isn&#39;t just communication—it&#39;s building. What breaks people is failing to build in some form, whether that&#39;s code, docs, or a newsletter. Those who lose motivation usually stopped treating their outlet as building anything at all.</div>

<div class="issue-story">
<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="/2026/08/09/stories/how-successful-companies-go-blind-cbc8bc/">How successful companies go blind</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="how-successful-companies-go-blind-cbc8bc"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">Successful companies can lose the ability to recognize good engineering the same way cave fish lost their sight: the environment stops rewarding the trait, so it quietly disappears. New hires learn broken systems, then replicate them through hiring panels, and careful engineers who push back get overruled until they leave or adapt.</div>

<div class="issue-story">
<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="/2026/08/09/stories/how-much-should-a-manager-code-a34c50/">How much should a manager code?</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="how-much-should-a-manager-code-a34c50"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">Coding as a manager isn&#39;t about hitting a time percentage. It&#39;s about staying connected to how your systems and team actually work. The real risk isn&#39;t coding too much or too little — it&#39;s coding to carry delivery yourself instead of building a team that runs without you. A green contribution graph isn&#39;t the goal. A team that barely needs you is.</div>

</div>
<div class="issue-story">
<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="/2026/08/09/stories/how-do-i-deal-with-my-team-member-resisting-a-change-d42c4f/">How do I deal with my team member resisting a change?</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="how-do-i-deal-with-my-team-member-resisting-a-change-d42c4f"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">Overt resistance to change is often more useful than it looks. The person objecting out loud is still engaged, and their pushback frequently signals a flaw in the plan, an unacknowledged cost, or simply exhaustion from too many changes at once. Research shows that perceived fairness, not enthusiasm or persuasion, most reliably reduces resistance.</div>
<div class="issue-story-meta">Leadership in Tech &middot; via aiste.ulozaite@gmail.com &middot; <a href="https://leadershipintech.com/subscribers/027d9985-d4c5-48a7-af59-5152efd9c8a4/unsubscribe">unsubscribe</a></div>
</div>
<div class="issue-story">
<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="/2026/08/09/stories/how-do-i-create-accountability-without-authority-113ff7/">How do I create accountability without authority?</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="how-do-i-create-accountability-without-authority-113ff7"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">Cross-functional leaders often assume that once a commitment is made in a meeting, it will hold. It rarely does without deliberate structure to support it. Accountability in a matrix organisation must be built through precise agreements, visible records, and early conversations about competing priorities. The goal is habits the whole group owns, not just the leader, so accountability survives long after any single person moves on.</div>
<div class="issue-story-meta">Leadership in Tech &middot; via aiste.ulozaite@gmail.com &middot; <a href="https://leadershipintech.com/subscribers/027d9985-d4c5-48a7-af59-5152efd9c8a4/unsubscribe">unsubscribe</a></div>
</div>
<div class="issue-story">
<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="/2026/08/09/stories/the-mario-meeting-7e8068/">The Mario meeting</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="the-mario-meeting-7e8068"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">Companies plan compensation budgets more than a year in advance, long before anyone sees their review. At any given time, two budgets run at once: one being spent now, one being built for next year. Senior leaders must understand this full process, not just the end result. Most employees only see the final number, but the real decisions happen much earlier, in rooms most people never enter.</div>

<div class="issue-story">
<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="/2026/08/09/stories/the-social-physics-of-conversation-02faac/">The social physics of conversation</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="the-social-physics-of-conversation-02faac"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">Research from MIT found that how groups communicate matters more than what they say. Tracking tone, turn-taking, and who speaks to whom predicted team performance better than intelligence or talent combined. Groups where conversation flows freely between all members consistently outperform those where exchanges funnel through one central person.</div>
<div class="issue-story-meta">Leadership in Tech &middot; via aiste.ulozaite@gmail.com &middot; <a href="https://leadershipintech.com/subscribers/027d9985-d4c5-48a7-af59-5152efd9c8a4/unsubscribe">unsubscribe</a></div>
</div>
<div class="issue-story">
<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="/2026/08/09/stories/how-to-ask-for-help-from-people-who-don-t-know-you-7a75b0/">How to ask for help from people who don&#39;t know you</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="how-to-ask-for-help-from-people-who-don-t-know-you-7a75b0"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">Asking for help is a skill anyone can learn. The key is to think from the other person&#39;s perspective. Make yourself worth helping by showing real proof of your work, keep your context brief, and make your request small and specific. Also make it easy for them to say no, since willing help builds lasting relationships while forced help destroys them.</div>
<div class="issue-story-meta">Leadership in Tech &middot; via aiste.ulozaite@gmail.com &middot; <a href="https://leadershipintech.com/subscribers/027d9985-d4c5-48a7-af59-5152efd9c8a4/unsubscribe">unsubscribe</a></div>
</div>
<div class="issue-story">
<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="/2026/08/09/stories/the-four-pillars-of-engineering-management-6ba0af/">The four pillars of engineering management</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="the-four-pillars-of-engineering-management-6ba0af"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">Engineering managers exist because growing organizations need coordination, alignment, and direction that flat structures cannot sustain. The role varies by company and context, but the core job stays the same: make the team effective. This breaks down into four areas — leading people, guiding technical decisions, shaping product thinking, and driving delivery. Success means understanding what blocks the team and removing those obstacles.</div>
<div class="issue-story-meta">Leadership in Tech &middot; via aiste.ulozaite@gmail.com &middot; <a href="https://leadershipintech.com/subscribers/027d9985-d4c5-48a7-af59-5152efd9c8a4/unsubscribe">unsubscribe</a></div>
</div>
<div class="issue-story">
<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="/2026/08/09/stories/how-meta-sets-up-super-ic-teams-a25cd2/">How Meta sets up super IC teams</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="how-meta-sets-up-super-ic-teams-a25cd2"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">Small, high-powered teams of experienced individual contributors are gaining popularity at companies trying to launch new products or transform operations. These &quot;super IC teams&quot; work best with four things in place: an executive sponsor with broad influence, full team dedication with no split responsibilities, a sharp focus on a real customer problem, and a handoff plan before scaling becomes urgent. Without these conditions, teams waste time on politics and lose momentum.</div>

<div class="issue-story-blurb"></div>

<div class="issue-story">
<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="/2026/08/09/stories/a-small-problem-731498/">A small problem</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="a-small-problem-731498"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">In 1982, Captain Eric Moody told passengers his 747 had &quot;a small problem&quot; after all four engines failed over the Indian Ocean. That understatement was deliberate: the cabin needed calm, while the cockpit got the full brutal truth. The lesson is that composure is something you broadcast, not just feel, and the message must match who receives it.</div>
<div class="issue-story-meta">Leadership in Tech &middot; via aiste.ulozaite@gmail.com &middot; <a href="https://leadershipintech.com/subscribers/027d9985-d4c5-48a7-af59-5152efd9c8a4/unsubscribe">unsubscribe</a></div>
</div>
<div class="issue-story">
<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="/2026/08/09/stories/what-does-playing-politics-mean-for-software-engineers-67d7c8/">What does &quot;playing politics&quot; mean for software engineers?</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="what-does-playing-politics-mean-for-software-engineers-67d7c8"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">Playing politics at work isn&#39;t about scheming. It means knowing who holds real power, avoiding conflict with them, and actively helping them succeed. When you disagree with a powerful person, do it privately, stay polite, and drop it when overruled. Make sure your contributions are visible, and respond quickly when senior people need help.</div>
<div class="issue-story-meta">Leadership in Tech &middot; via aiste.ulozaite@gmail.com &middot; <a href="https://leadershipintech.com/subscribers/027d9985-d4c5-48a7-af59-5152efd9c8a4/unsubscribe">unsubscribe</a></div>
</div>
<div class="issue-story">
<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="/2026/08/09/stories/effectiveness-comes-from-developing-both-leadership-and-mana-a96e53/">Effectiveness comes from developing both leadership and management skills</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="effectiveness-comes-from-developing-both-leadership-and-mana-a96e53"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">Leadership and management aren&#39;t opposites — they&#39;re both necessary. Leadership creates direction and inspires movement, while management turns that movement into real results. One without the other leads to either vision without execution or busy work without purpose. The good news is that you&#39;re already practicing both, since every time you influence others or make smart use of limited resources, you&#39;re doing exactly that.</div>
<div class="issue-story-meta">Leadership in Tech &middot; via aiste.ulozaite@gmail.com &middot; <a href="https://leadershipintech.com/subscribers/027d9985-d4c5-48a7-af59-5152efd9c8a4/unsubscribe">unsubscribe</a></div>
</div>
<div class="issue-story">
<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="/2026/08/09/stories/avoid-the-trap-of-magical-thinking-59c80e/">Avoid the trap of magical thinking</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="avoid-the-trap-of-magical-thinking-59c80e"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">Good leadership isn&#39;t about inspiring speeches or chasing productivity hacks. It&#39;s about understanding real business challenges, removing bottlenecks, and focusing teams on what actually matters. Magical thinking lets leaders avoid responsibility, so when things fail, they blame effort rather than direction. Strong teams built on clear goals and good communication outperform collections of star individuals.</div>
<div class="issue-story-meta">Leadership in Tech &middot; via aiste.ulozaite@gmail.com &middot; <a href="https://leadershipintech.com/subscribers/027d9985-d4c5-48a7-af59-5152efd9c8a4/unsubscribe">unsubscribe</a></div>
</div>
<div class="issue-story">
<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="/2026/08/09/stories/engineering-leaders-day-to-day-activities-dbb6f8/">Engineering leaders day-to-day activities</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="engineering-leaders-day-to-day-activities-dbb6f8"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb"></div>
<div class="issue-story-meta">Leadership in Tech &middot; via aiste.ulozaite@gmail.com &middot; <a href="https://leadershipintech.com/subscribers/027d9985-d4c5-48a7-af59-5152efd9c8a4/unsubscribe">unsubscribe</a></div>
</div>
<div class="issue-story">
<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="https://leadershipintech.com/links/22841/027d9985-d4c5-48a7-af59-5152efd9c8a4/email">The best prioritization is no prioritization</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="the-best-prioritization-is-no-prioritization-e0e339"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">Prioritization frameworks waste time and rarely prove their worth. Instead, build faster: speed beats clever decision-making every time, and shipping more teaches you what works. Keep teams focused on one area permanently so decisions stay simple and comparable. Treat resourcing as your main lever, not constant replanning.</div>
<div class="issue-story-meta">Leadership in Tech &middot; via aiste.ulozaite@gmail.com &middot; <a href="https://leadershipintech.com/subscribers/027d9985-d4c5-48a7-af59-5152efd9c8a4/unsubscribe">unsubscribe</a></div>
</div>
<div class="issue-story">
<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="/2026/08/09/stories/the-antithesis-principle-fcd07e/">The antithesis principle</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="the-antithesis-principle-fcd07e"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">Most people notice how human tendencies like needing entertainment, following charisma, or judging first impressions affect others. The smarter move is also turning that observation inward and working to eliminate those same tendencies in yourself. This is the Antithesis Principle: truths about human nature point both outward as tactics and inward as warnings. Smart people find the tactic. Wise people also find the warning.</div>
<div class="issue-story-meta">Leadership in Tech &middot; via aiste.ulozaite@gmail.com &middot; <a href="https://leadershipintech.com/subscribers/027d9985-d4c5-48a7-af59-5152efd9c8a4/unsubscribe">unsubscribe</a></div>
</div>
<div class="issue-story">
<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="/2026/08/09/stories/framework-for-managers-starting-a-new-role-b2beaa/">Framework for managers starting a new role</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="framework-for-managers-starting-a-new-role-b2beaa"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">Starting strong in a new management role is less about moving fast and more about building the right foundation. Focus on absorbing company culture, building relationships early, and aligning with your manager on clear expectations. Resist the urge to prove yourself immediately. Instead, listen to your team, spot patterns, and let your first wins come from solving problems that actually matter.</div>
<div class="issue-story-meta">Leadership in Tech &middot; via aiste.ulozaite@gmail.com &middot; <a href="https://leadershipintech.com/subscribers/027d9985-d4c5-48a7-af59-5152efd9c8a4/unsubscribe">unsubscribe</a></div>
</div>
<div class="issue-story">
<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="/2026/08/09/stories/how-to-think-about-span-of-control-25e120/">How to think about span of control</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="how-to-think-about-span-of-control-25e120"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">Span of control and team size involve real tradeoffs. Fewer management layers reduce complexity and improve clarity, but overloaded managers neglect their teams and slow everything down. Small teams of around eight people tend to move faster and work better together, while larger teams offer more resilience. As companies grow and processes mature, spans of control can increase, but mandates pushing managers to oversee 20 or more people rarely work well in practice.</div>
<div class="issue-story-meta">Leadership in Tech &middot; via aiste.ulozaite@gmail.com &middot; <a href="https://leadershipintech.com/subscribers/027d9985-d4c5-48a7-af59-5152efd9c8a4/unsubscribe">unsubscribe</a></div>
</div>
<div class="issue-story">
<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="/2026/08/09/stories/no-wind-is-a-good-wind-ca01f5/">No wind is a good wind</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="no-wind-is-a-good-wind-ca01f5"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">Teams move fast but build the wrong things when leadership lacks a clear vision. There&#39;s a simple check: ask yourself what the vision is, then ask a few engineers the same question and see if the answers match. If the vision exists but you&#39;re not part of those conversations, push your way into the right rooms. If it doesn&#39;t exist at all, that&#39;s a problem worth raising loudly.</div>
<div class="issue-story-meta">Leadership in Tech &middot; via aiste.ulozaite@gmail.com &middot; <a href="https://leadershipintech.com/subscribers/027d9985-d4c5-48a7-af59-5152efd9c8a4/unsubscribe">unsubscribe</a></div>
</div>
<div class="issue-story">
<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="https://leadershipintech.com/links/22895/027d9985-d4c5-48a7-af59-5152efd9c8a4/email">The best prioritization is no prioritization</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="the-best-prioritization-is-no-prioritization-84b823"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb"></div>
<div class="issue-story-meta">Leadership in Tech &middot; via aiste.ulozaite@gmail.com &middot; <a href="https://leadershipintech.com/subscribers/027d9985-d4c5-48a7-af59-5152efd9c8a4/unsubscribe">unsubscribe</a></div>
</div>
<div class="issue-story">
<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="/2026/08/09/stories/before-you-delegate-125424/">Before you delegate</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="before-you-delegate-125424"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">Delegating is hard when explaining a task feels slower than just doing it yourself. Wes shares a simple checklist of six questions that makes it easier: understand what the person already knows, share the why, gather needed resources, show what good looks like, set a clear timeline, and flag likely risks. These questions help build shared context fast and improve the quality of work handed off.</div>
<div class="issue-story-meta">Leadership in Tech &middot; via aiste.ulozaite@gmail.com &middot; <a href="https://leadershipintech.com/subscribers/027d9985-d4c5-48a7-af59-5152efd9c8a4/unsubscribe">unsubscribe</a></div>
</div>
<div class="issue-story">
<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="https://leadershipintech.com/links/22933/027d9985-d4c5-48a7-af59-5152efd9c8a4/email">Overpromising</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="overpromising-7a680d"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">Promising career rewards to keep team members happy often backfires badly. The initial boost fades fast, and if the promise falls through, trust is shattered and can take years to rebuild. Managers should only make promises they can fully guarantee, and caveat anything uncertain with honest disclaimers. Focus on what you can control, not what you hope will happen.</div>
<div class="issue-story-meta">Leadership in Tech &middot; via aiste.ulozaite@gmail.com &middot; <a href="https://leadershipintech.com/subscribers/027d9985-d4c5-48a7-af59-5152efd9c8a4/unsubscribe">unsubscribe</a></div>
</div>
<div class="issue-story">
<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="/2026/08/09/stories/engineering-management-after-the-cost-of-code-collapsed-fd9f5a/">Engineering management after the cost of code collapsed</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="engineering-management-after-the-cost-of-code-collapsed-fd9f5a"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">Engineering management rules aren&#39;t uniformly obsolete or valid in the AI era. The key is checking what assumption each practice rests on. If a rule depends on the cost of writing code, revisit it. If it depends on how humans coordinate, verify, or accept risk, it still holds. Code generation got cheap, but specification, judgment, and ownership did not. Whoever has to sign a decision still sets the pace.</div>
<div class="issue-story-meta">Leadership in Tech &middot; via aiste.ulozaite@gmail.com &middot; <a href="https://leadershipintech.com/subscribers/027d9985-d4c5-48a7-af59-5152efd9c8a4/unsubscribe">unsubscribe</a></div>
</div>
<div class="issue-story">
<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="/2026/08/09/stories/when-nothing-happens-b1719a/">When nothing happens</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="when-nothing-happens-b1719a"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">Organizations don&#39;t punish bad behavior — they punish bad behavior when results turn. Research shows that whether a harsh leader is seen as inspiring or abusive depends mostly on whether the team is winning. Cases like Steve Jobs, Linda Wachner, and Bobby Knight all follow the same pattern: the behavior stays constant, but the moment results drop, tolerance evaporates. The real question for any leader is how much cover strong performance is currently buying for someone on their team.</div>
<div class="issue-story-meta">Leadership in Tech &middot; via aiste.ulozaite@gmail.com &middot; <a href="https://leadershipintech.com/subscribers/027d9985-d4c5-48a7-af59-5152efd9c8a4/unsubscribe">unsubscribe</a></div>
</div>
<div class="issue-story">
<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="https://link.mail.beehiiv.com/ss/c/u001.u7AEjFG7xD7PeXgObD5uI1o6BtYZXOmqEviUlhsqJwh5AVQIa6Tht9vCY02wzMBPG2OWhpuSeG1pH6gI70LJkOgW3ReFcJi_KtU0UzohCzbpU9MCaTidx0qcYDBL23iT2-mX0QAOUlSsNJVFFgDqrqMVoNt1t0FcaBv_zTMTJp4/4si/c3Fv450_TjO6jsHLxm7gVg/h7/h001.ZRD_nVevPDKIz5eLkppH4jT9-orWBqhsFNI-VAwFXsQ">A Small Problem</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="a-small-problem-45dc43"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">In an organization you are always broadcasting on more than one channel at once, whether you intend to or not. Your exec team needs the unvarnished situation. The wider company needs enough to stay oriented without spinning out. Customers need a different message again. The failure mode is not the absence of a calm voice. It is using one voice for everyone: either feeding the cockpit a soothing story or dumping the cockpit&#39;s panic straight into the cabin. Calm is contagious, and so is fear. Whichever one you transmit is the one that spreads.</div>

<div class="issue-story">
<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="https://link.mail.beehiiv.com/ss/c/u001.GWllEGwX7fbrlXfChzlURqc0yGRH1oFUfM9uHWImYDHYbb17FpsTGoSInLkPtk4vmdjKuSeGSWI5xeOktqD5973jxQ2UY56WiMbkxaLw7L6x2Arh3-A90eUibI1NcuyLJgVjQYYMJUMQl1RpJPgNuU84Gvi7TFyZMxZKSqF-5-QPJaNMc0IePetStVOwA_iZ/4si/c3Fv450_TjO6jsHLxm7gVg/h10/h001.JDCwnizboDYrG7dYfMvyiNc182MDGGem39CYgFgStow">How Successful Companies Go Blind</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="how-successful-companies-go-blind-30b37c"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">Companies who have forgotten what it took to become successful are similar: they stop recognizing competence, because the environment has stopped expressing the trait in anyone the company hires. Call it competence blindness, which is different from incumbents who fail because they cling to the customers and margins of yesterday&#39;s market. Firms with competence blindness do not disappear. In fact, they can survive for decades.</div>

<div class="issue-story">
<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="https://link.mail.beehiiv.com/ss/c/u001.3a5P_SwQzY5x8USD2q4p0t6xejYHTQLoRuIuwxU-yMFOzEQIYjDfzH5uE-88-eQcCyck73FiHRwkh8UJ-paafOAM_9DDmK5TS3beS-DBuu_hOSBeyWmGlas7Tu_RwFTkcVQeJrq6DwlPEyC0OAMCI3mVHf2yzNUVi-eZJOp6Z9I/4sp/EVxvD-HVT8KkXqeJSLbjnw/h8/h001.PqcS_o1l9vrG3atpFcv9xDv4oqbCc-dIVqIH8TPRqjg">Mission To Metrics</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="mission-to-metrics-6151cd"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">Mission to Metrics is a written cascade, running from why the org exists down to the numbers that measure whether it&#39;s getting there. Every team&#39;s plan is a more detailed version of one slice of the layer above. Every metric traces back to the mission. Once it&#39;s written, anyone in the org can read the page and understand how their work connects to the company mission.</div>

<div class="issue-story">
<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="/2026/08/09/stories/the-vital-few-9ef6d3/">The Vital Few</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="the-vital-few-9ef6d3"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">Stewardship is the actual work. It means giving your strongest people air cover, not just assignments. It means defending their focus from the steady drizzle of interruptions that drifts toward competence. It means offering them growth rather than merely more volume, and fighting the reflex to drop the next critical thing on the proven desk simply because it is proven.</div>

<div class="issue-story">
<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="https://link.mail.beehiiv.com/ss/c/u001.WjplEBHoYsVAvmS39jW9CS22XTcGP5UZ9HVI-2T4L_OQ3ggRDc07iX7QJzoC01X1UNfjVBqRCRuqhGn5eBJKIbs4si_lT8-HxWbJhSoP-6X-A-HH69HUZkce_YTn_MaI4jwJUeAvUBFL-VNITl2Lsu5bmnext5xRcEOlCNHxYRs/4sp/EVxvD-HVT8KkXqeJSLbjnw/h11/h001.GH9jPVAij-VMa-0luoCFTopRvoYJ6wza-isTsVOhrYU">Make No Assumptions</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="make-no-assumptions-80843f"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">When that architect leaves, they are replaced by someone who aspires to operate in the same code layer, but simply cannot because they lack enough context to do so. As a result, that new replacement creates a new code layer, despite not intending to. If the team runs through a handful of folks as the new team leads struggle, it&#39;s easy to end up with a complex code horizon very quickly.</div>

<div class="issue-story">
<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="https://link.mail.beehiiv.com/ss/c/u001.K3FzaWG5vnes9asChrWWUKvb9dwzjn1HQSwhuZBUxV35J1BSJz3kn0akZMiyPRaF29mELX0KsR-Mirjxie4PKoCehlS1PNzrA0nDcH8Z0ZVdKGW2F8uXU34Sin3r8Ww36r2b7pt0Qlw4KotvRwr1T3DCilZ8QjVqsL0iYcKNbuk2k6_HZUF6ThygwmXQCgRK/4sl/h7_ivzkxSm-YefoUD5CPxQ/h8/h001.fdfp__p78HOtni5uNaTR-pDbQfHLex5OfBSRiXqAfaE">The Productivity-Experience Paradox</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="the-productivity-experience-paradox-aa3d5f"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">If experience and productivity really are decoupling with AI, will leaders still be able to justify caring about developer experience? I really hope so, but the case for investing in it was always that it paid off in output, and that&#39;s exactly what&#39;s now up for debate.</div>

<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="/2026/08/09/stories/the-art-of-dealing-with-ambiguity-4b0a11/">The Art Of Dealing With Ambiguity</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="the-art-of-dealing-with-ambiguity-4b0a11"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">The magic happens when you reframe ambiguity as an opportunity to define the problem space. Once you&#39;ve established clear boundaries, success metrics, and constraints, the fog lifts, and the path forward materializes.</div>

<div class="issue-story">
<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="https://link.mail.beehiiv.com/ss/c/u001.yC9eJs5Q3flddlsql05gYdOm7DJmwzIcX2w6eS_B24yRD6kk5po7-pma3CAlINf9u29yuPeSKTcPu3vQinTh4vqgeA1k9Gda8QtEXWI7Fuxb3Ishl0u_xabooyf7ineZdInqCl4NzYM_ua5BcW6GfEUK8XyWj_Lz5zFGMoeR_o45FCyDD5Dflc-ROD3b6UYe/4sl/h7_ivzkxSm-YefoUD5CPxQ/h11/h001.12npniWQvMWwvg7id1_hylgbw37dWta3dnydKDuMlAQ">Defensible, But Not Good</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="defensible-but-not-good-f00773"></span></h3>

<div class="issue-story">
<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="https://link.mail.beehiiv.com/ss/c/u001.u7AEjFG7xD7PeXgObD5uI68iIspGEhbpwFIn8knMoQ-1xQtzy2mUp1DnCvnJ-Lm6nW23E7aPp2eau2tOUh5Jh9jllXYzAvNv-JjLugochlbXNQCVwLbg04QYuhpfFyXmyi1s1y4IMfWD5KiE_3HStGQwgUyUilJuzRhPzFZGAieyi0lW_4OxW9bUkLcSiRxqyZ74cxQA46IWz8DdziLJPA/4sw/eBYeDbHiRy6heWHd_5s8-A/h10/h001.-QnvUinzYpeEwfG1Ki_yqlKGrCW2gvR4_GeHsDHVipQ">The Conductor Developer</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="the-conductor-developer-80d256"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">A great conductor is first and foremost a great musician. They could play the instruments themselves. That&#39;s not why they&#39;re standing on the podium. Their value comes from understanding the whole score. The orchestra doesn&#39;t need the conductor because the musicians aren&#39;t talented enough. It needs the conductor because someone has to hold the whole system in their head. Increasingly, I think that&#39;s what great software developers are doing.</div>

<div class="issue-story-meta">uloza+hey@proton.me &middot; <a href="https://softwareleadweekly.us6.list-manage.com/unsubscribe?u=1a258e0fefbb23214c59c5a8d&amp;id=efe3d3cd5b&amp;t=b&amp;e=86169b0ba2&amp;c=b23acd1ae1">unsubscribe</a></div>

<h2 class="issue-category-label">Writing</h2>
<div class="issue-story-grid issue-story-grid-single">
<div class="issue-story">
<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="/2026/08/09/stories/don-t-be-a-meat-proxy-7df677/">Don&#39;t Be a Meat Proxy</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="don-t-be-a-meat-proxy-7df677"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">Verbose writing is often lazy writing. Copying it straight from the output you got from AI is an even worse type of laziness. Writing a response in your own words isn&#39;t about politeness, it&#39;s a decent certificate that you actually read.</div>
<div class="issue-story-meta">uloza+hey@proton.me &middot; <a href="https://softwareleadweekly.us6.list-manage.com/unsubscribe?u=1a258e0fefbb23214c59c5a8d&amp;id=efe3d3cd5b&amp;t=b&amp;e=86169b0ba2&amp;c=b23acd1ae1">unsubscribe</a></div>
</div>
</div>
</div>
<div class="issue-category-rule"></div>
<div class="issue-index-num">05</div>
<div class="issue-category-body">
<h2 class="issue-category-label">AI</h2>
<div class="issue-story-grid">
<div class="issue-story">
<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="/2026/08/09/stories/what-s-gone-wrong-with-ai-labor-a-thought-experiment-70601b/">What&#39;s Gone Wrong With AI &amp; Labor — A Thought Experiment</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="what-s-gone-wrong-with-ai-labor-a-thought-experiment-70601b"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">Coding agents haven&#39;t replaced engineers because the software industry open-sourced every intermediate artifact, from specs to pull requests to code reviews, giving agents a training set that teaches collaboration instead of just imitation. Every profession chasing its own AI moment should be asking whether it can manufacture that same missing middle.</div>
<div class="issue-story-meta">uloza+hey@proton.me &middot; <a href="https://softwareleadweekly.us6.list-manage.com/unsubscribe?u=1a258e0fefbb23214c59c5a8d&amp;id=efe3d3cd5b&amp;t=b&amp;e=86169b0ba2&amp;c=b23acd1ae1">unsubscribe</a></div>
</div>
<div class="issue-story">
<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="/2026/08/09/stories/escaping-the-llm-coding-rat-race-a8a624/">Escaping the LLM Coding Rat Race</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="escaping-the-llm-coding-rat-race-a8a624"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">The AI speed race isn&#39;t producing happier customers: 30% increase in total supply; 3% growth in utilization. This suggests the bottleneck isn&#39;t shipping speed but effective distribution and adoption.</div>
<div class="issue-story-meta">uloza+hey@proton.me &middot; <a href="https://softwareleadweekly.us6.list-manage.com/unsubscribe?u=1a258e0fefbb23214c59c5a8d&amp;id=efe3d3cd5b&amp;t=b&amp;e=86169b0ba2&amp;c=b23acd1ae1">unsubscribe</a></div>
</div>
<div class="issue-story">
<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="https://leadershipintech.com/links/22749/027d9985-d4c5-48a7-af59-5152efd9c8a4/email">The AI Testing Gap Between Demo and Production</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="the-ai-testing-gap-between-demo-and-production-c0c8b7"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">A model that works in a demo and one your team trusts on release day are two different things. At the QA Leadership Summit, leaders from companies like Google Cloud and Salesforce walkthrough how they&#39;re putting AI into testing, measuring its impact, and building reliable systems that scale. Free virtual event, July 22.</div>
<div class="issue-story-meta">Leadership in Tech &middot; Sponsored &middot; via aiste.ulozaite@gmail.com &middot; <a href="https://leadershipintech.com/subscribers/027d9985-d4c5-48a7-af59-5152efd9c8a4/unsubscribe">unsubscribe</a></div>
</div>
<div class="issue-story">
<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="https://leadershipintech.com/links/22735/027d9985-d4c5-48a7-af59-5152efd9c8a4/email">[Webinar] 8 levels of context maturity in AI-native development</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="webinar-8-levels-of-context-maturity-in-ai-native-developmen-55c57d"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">AI is in your engineering workflow. While the token spend shows it, the throughput doesn&#39;t. The human is very much still in the loop, and that&#39;s a context problem. Join live (FREE) on Jul 23 to see the 8 levels of context maturity: where most teams are stuck, what the ceiling looks like at each stage, and what it actually takes to make the most out of your agents.</div>

<div class="issue-story">
<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="/2026/08/09/stories/notes-on-the-software-factory-6a9181/">Notes on the software factory</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="notes-on-the-software-factory-6a9181"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">AI coding agents work best when given real tools like browser access, deployment pipelines, and test runners. Adding a critic agent to review work improves quality, and pairing that with more thinking time on hard problems helps further. Memory and long-running workflows are still weak points. The whole stack, from cloud infrastructure to IDEs, needs to be rebuilt around agents rather than adapted from human-centered tools.</div>

<div class="issue-story">
<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="/2026/08/09/stories/raise-the-ambition-threshold-94d017/">Raise the ambition threshold</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="raise-the-ambition-threshold-94d017"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">Building software faster with AI doesn&#39;t mean building more software. Every new system adds ongoing costs to operate, secure, and maintain, and too many obligations can crowd out the capacity to build what truly matters. AI should raise the bar for what teams attempt, not lower the bar for what gets built. Companies that use AI only to clear backlogs risk being overtaken by bolder competitors tackling harder, more valuable problems.</div>

<div class="issue-story-blurb">Offloading thinking to AI is increasingly common, from trivial daily choices to complex reasoning. While this can free up time and boost productivity, Yennie argues it risks eroding our autonomy and ability to think independently. Using AI to check and extend our own thinking differs from replacing that thinking entirely. The real question is whether we are automating routine work or giving away our own judgment and desires.</div>

<div class="issue-story-blurb">Codebases often build up distinct layers over time as new developers add code without fully understanding what came before. AI tools are making this worse, and the same problem is now spreading to how teams reason and make decisions. AI-generated analysis can look polished while being built on flawed data, leading teams to solve problems that don&#39;t exist. The fix is to inspect every layer of reasoning rather than accepting it at face value.</div>

<div class="issue-story-blurb">AI speeds up coding, but coding is only a small part of most developers&#39; days. Senior engineers spend most of their time in meetings, reviews, and system design, areas where AI helps little. This means AI saves seniors around 15% of their time, while juniors, who code more, gain closer to 25%. Expect steady improvements, but not dramatic productivity leaps, especially from your most experienced staff.</div>

<div class="issue-story-blurb">Building context and memory for production agents usually requires managing separate vector databases, caching layers, and session stores. This fragmented stack introduces latency and massive engineering overhead. Redis Iris fixes this by unifying the context layer at runtime with semantic search, persistent agent state, and caching into a single engine. Clone the demo repo to see the code in action.</div>

<div class="issue-story-blurb">Tools like Claude Code, ChatGPT, and Cursor look like magic from the outside. You type a message, and the AI reads your files, fixes bugs, runs tests. Under the hood, it&#39;s a stack of pieces, and once you see them, the behavior of these tools gets a lot more predictable. Nemanja&#39;s walkthrough builds that stack from scratch, starting from the simplest possible interaction and adding layers until we arrive at something like Claude Code.</div>

<div class="issue-story-blurb">Coding agents are stateless, your context isn&#39;t. Jolli Memory stores agent memory as Git-backed files in your repo, so it persists across sessions and follows you between Cursor, Claude Code, Codex, and VS Code. Ships with the codebase to every teammate. Free and open source.</div>

<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="https://programmingdigest.net/links/22802/91ee7b33-c28b-4a8e-bab4-e5b8bc4f3f47/email">In-house LLM serving at Netflix</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="in-house-llm-serving-at-netflix-9a8768"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">Netflix runs its own LLM serving stack on top of vLLM and NVIDIA Triton, integrated into existing production infrastructure rather than a separate system. Key decisions around engine choice, model packaging, API design, and deployment strategies each revealed unexpected trade-offs only under real production load. A notable example is constrained decoding, where per-request CPU processing caused latency to grow linearly with batch size until a rewrite using vLLM V1&#39;s batch-level API and C++ solved the bottleneck.</div>
<div class="issue-story-meta">Programming Digest &middot; via aiste.ulozaite@gmail.com &middot; <a href="https://programmingdigest.net/subscribers/91ee7b33-c28b-4a8e-bab4-e5b8bc4f3f47/unsubscribe">unsubscribe</a></div>

<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="https://programmingdigest.net/links/22856/91ee7b33-c28b-4a8e-bab4-e5b8bc4f3f47/email">Stop re-explaining your codebase to your AI agent every morning</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="stop-re-explaining-your-codebase-to-your-ai-agent-every-morn-c5dcfb"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">Coding agents are stateless, your context isn&#39;t. Jolli Memory stores agent memory as Git-backed files in your repo, so it persists across sessions and follows you between Cursor, Claude Code, Codex, and VS Code. Ships with the codebase to every teammate. Free and open source.</div>

<div class="issue-story">
<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="https://programmingdigest.net/links/22857/91ee7b33-c28b-4a8e-bab4-e5b8bc4f3f47/email">Harness engineering deep dive</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="harness-engineering-deep-dive-b1a5b1"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">The model is just a small part of a real AI agent system. The harness — the loop, tools, sandbox, state, sensors, and guardrails — is where most of the engineering actually lives. Sensors should transform tool output into clean signals; the agent should never decide it is done on its own; and action spaces shrink through tool design, not longer instructions. The outer harness is the durable asset that compounds over time, while models get swapped and commoditized.</div>
<div class="issue-story-meta">Programming Digest &middot; via aiste.ulozaite@gmail.com &middot; <a href="https://programmingdigest.net/subscribers/91ee7b33-c28b-4a8e-bab4-e5b8bc4f3f47/unsubscribe">unsubscribe</a></div>
</div>
<div class="issue-story">
<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="/2026/08/09/stories/how-llms-figure-out-what-you-mean-643f36/">How LLMs figure out what you mean</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="how-llms-figure-out-what-you-mean-643f36"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">An AI can&#39;t work with raw text, so it converts each word into a list of numbers called a vector. Think of each vector as an arrow, where words with similar meanings point in similar directions. The model then scores how closely any two arrows align and converts those scores into percentage weights, showing how much attention each word pays to every other word.</div>
<div class="issue-story-meta">Programming Digest &middot; via aiste.ulozaite@gmail.com &middot; <a href="https://programmingdigest.net/subscribers/91ee7b33-c28b-4a8e-bab4-e5b8bc4f3f47/unsubscribe">unsubscribe</a></div>
</div>
<div class="issue-story">
<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="https://programmingdigest.net/links/22960/91ee7b33-c28b-4a8e-bab4-e5b8bc4f3f47/email">Ship long-horizon agents that hold up in production</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="ship-long-horizon-agents-that-hold-up-in-production-9a402e"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">Long-running agents break the assumptions behind standard app infrastructure. They can run for minutes or hours, pause for human input, recover from worker crashes, and still need controls around cost, memory, code execution, and tracing. Download the engineering guide to the runtime primitives that keep production agents reliable.</div>

<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="https://link.mail.beehiiv.com/ss/c/u001.3a5P_SwQzY5x8USD2q4p0vHb8GdcjonmgvPcPB9XhFMDdVI1o_GtDhuc7TimzwJdcjZzJeWmzhM46tdeEQcNNX-Lv_WZERf8JkcpW8TV7TX7sqS1Bb998JFhNuMrFHzAAKDCIAaqi1A8kaRVq5cO1eyvzfN75qTQfKTytp4jSjarU2YOtq2XrkjJi8kyF8GSkEVW2eCTk34RG3zFUazkN4jxdlY3tE2hGtrr6KyGgGuLByoTpiY1WRDZb8SUoEoQ2F-pq3mD6HRrto9HKZxE-w/4se/fAcrFrAxSxK8wmcqipcb6Q/h3/h001.OFmQRDGBA0X089QY10kxloBSQcQo1e03_Fvd8vbH6KY">Free Gauntlet AI Night School: MCP Factory — Build Any MCP You Want</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="free-gauntlet-ai-night-school-mcp-factory-build-any-mcp-you--72952f"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">Aaron Gallant breaks down MCP Factory, a repository that lets you spin up any MCP server you want instead of hand-building each integration from scratch. MCP servers are how AI agents plug into real tools and data, and Aaron walks through how the repo is structured, how to go from an idea to a working MCP fast, and how generating them changes what you can wire your agents into. With the next cohort approaching, it&#39;s also a clear look at the kind of work Gauntlet engineers do every day. You&#39;ll learn: (1) Why MCP servers matter for connecting agents to real tools and data. (2) How MCP Factory is structured as a reusable repository. (3) How to go from an idea to a working MCP quickly. (4) Practical patterns you can take back to your own projects.</div>

<div class="issue-story-blurb">Building context and memory for production AI agents usually means stitching together a fragile tool zoo of vector dbs, sync pipelines, and external session caches. <div class="issue-story">
<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="/2026/08/09/stories/using-local-coding-agents-b1fde5/">Using Local Coding Agents</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="using-local-coding-agents-b1fde5"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">This article is a tutorial on setting up a production-ready coding agent with a fully local stack. We will use a locally served LLM together with a local coding harness that can read files, make edits, run commands, and verify changes as shown in the figure above.</div>

<div class="issue-story-blurb">AI is in your engineering workflow. While the token spend shows it, the throughput doesn&#39;t. The human is very much still in the loop, and that&#39;s a context problem. This free webinar maps the 8 levels of context maturity: where most teams are stuck, what the ceiling looks like at each stage, and what it actually takes to make the most out of your agents. Join live July 23 (FREE).</div>

<div class="issue-story-blurb">Night School is Gauntlet&#39;s free education series on what AI-native work actually looks like — no hype, no vendor pitches, just engineers and operators showing how they really build. The full library is now available on demand, so you can watch any session whenever you want. Go deep on planning, specs, and decision logs that separate AI engineers from vibe coders. See how a team ships go-to-market work with AI in the loop, how to build any MCP you want, and how to make a real game with AI-generated art and no dev background. Every session is a practical, over-the-shoulder look at how AI changes the work — recorded and ready to watch on your schedule.</div>

<div class="issue-story-blurb">In this post we upgrade every piece of our basic harness toward that production shape, without hiding any of the mechanics behind a framework. The guiding question for the whole exercise is a simple one: How do you turn a single LLM call into a reliable system that can plan, act, recover, and prove it did the right thing?</div>

<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="/2026/08/09/stories/goldman-sees-ai-reshaping-indian-work-885539/">Goldman Sees AI Reshaping Indian Work</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="goldman-sees-ai-reshaping-indian-work-885539"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">Goldman Sachs estimates generative AI could replace 8–12% of India&#39;s non-agricultural jobs while complementing 42–48% by automating routine tasks and boosting productivity. Services such as education, media, finance, and professional work face the most exposure, while construction and other physical occupations remain relatively insulated, pointing to broad labor reallocation rather than mass unemployment over time.</div>

<div class="issue-story">
<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="/2026/08/09/stories/ai-traffic-favors-buying-pages-e558ec/">AI Traffic Favors Buying Pages</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="ai-traffic-favors-buying-pages-e558ec"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">WebFX analyzed nearly 600,000 AI referral sessions across 2,500 URLs and found that AI sends users mostly to decision-stage content, not awareness pages. Homepages led with 31.3%, while product, service, blog, and FAQ pages captured most remaining traffic. Pages with specific facts, pricing, citations, and expert signals performed best, suggesting AI rewards content that helps buyers act.</div>

<div class="issue-story">
<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="/2026/08/09/stories/ai-cracks-outsourcing-s-revenue-engine-f4bbba/">AI Cracks Outsourcing&#39;s Revenue Engine</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="ai-cracks-outsourcing-s-revenue-engine-f4bbba"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">Indian IT outsourcing giant Infosys cut its fiscal 2027 growth forecast to 1.5–3.0%, acknowledging that customers are shifting technology work toward AI. The warning matters beyond one company: outsourcing was built on expanding teams and billable effort, but AI lets clients internalize more capability, compress project scope, and spend less on labor-heavy services, weakening the industry&#39;s traditional revenue engine.</div>

<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="/2026/08/09/stories/agents-scale-sleep-discovery-a3d812/">Agents Scale Sleep Discovery</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="agents-scale-sleep-discovery-a3d812"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">Researchers built AI Sleep Co-Scientist, an expert-guided system that analyzed roughly 124,000 sleep studies and more than 50 TB of physiological signals. By automating labor-intensive preprocessing, hypothesis development, and statistical analysis while keeping humans in review.</div>

<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="https://newsletter.myclaw.ai/r/4bf08f96?m=2eaf3154-2af3-4e54-b574-fba62b0fa39d">Amazon Tests Health Agents</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="amazon-tests-health-agents-44a891"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">Amazon introduced PatientAgentBench, a clinician-vetted framework that creates synthetic patient records and multiturn conversations to test healthcare AI agents on safety, triage, workflows, and task completion. Across thousands of evaluations, even frontier models missed crisis resources, fabricated clinical details, and mishandled routine requests hiding serious risks.</div>

<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="/2026/08/09/stories/zuckerberg-bets-on-personal-agents-25b259/">Zuckerberg Bets on Personal Agents</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="zuckerberg-bets-on-personal-agents-25b259"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">Meta CEO Mark Zuckerberg predicts billions of people will use personal AI agents within five years to manage finances, health, relationships, and households through platforms like WhatsApp. He says these agents will underpin Meta&#39;s next products and revenue, even as AI infrastructure spending surges, free cash flow falls 91%, and Reality Labs&#39; cumulative losses reach roughly $88 billion.</div>

<div class="issue-story">
<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="/2026/08/09/stories/the-box-checking-agent-a6aca5/">The Box-Checking Agent</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="the-box-checking-agent-a6aca5"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">Mark Cuban says his Shark Tank company Rebel Cheese is saving $50,000 a month with a simple AI agent that photographs shipping boxes and invoices, checks sizes against carrier price lists, and automatically files credit requests when charges are wrong. The lesson is not flashy automation, but agents quietly finding money in boring operational gaps.</div>

<div class="issue-story">
<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="/2026/08/09/stories/hiring-s-algorithmic-blackball-acd5a8/">Hiring&#39;s Algorithmic Blackball</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="hiring-s-algorithmic-blackball-acd5a8"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">Stanford-led researchers found AI hiring vendors can turn one bad assessment into repeated rejection, as stored scores follow applicants across employers using the same platform for up to 330 days. Their Pymetrics audit found lost job advances and racial disparities, warning that opaque hiring systems can quietly blackball candidates before any human review, even across unrelated roles.</div>
<div class="issue-story-meta">MyClaw Newsletter &middot; via aiste.ulozaite@gmail.com &middot; <a href="https://newsletter.myclaw.ai/unsubscribe/?uuid=2eaf3154-2af3-4e54-b574-fba62b0fa39d&amp;key=dc9420fa06ad8715ca418803968586b4a3d508870a709a3c794ae1e1640114bf&amp;newsletter=deed1d04-92d6-4e2e-bf4e-5a395b641657">unsubscribe</a></div>

<div class="issue-story">
<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="/2026/08/09/stories/agentic-commerce-takes-cart-e903da/">Agentic Commerce Takes Cart</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="agentic-commerce-takes-cart-e903da"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">McKinsey says AI agents could mediate $3 trillion to $5 trillion in consumer commerce by 2030, moving shopping from human persuasion to machine-readable competition. As agents compare prices, fill carts, and eventually negotiate with store bots, retailers that expose inventory, pricing, and return data through APIs will win while human-only marketing loses power inside retail.</div>
<div class="issue-story-meta">MyClaw Newsletter &middot; via aiste.ulozaite@gmail.com &middot; <a href="https://newsletter.myclaw.ai/unsubscribe/?uuid=2eaf3154-2af3-4e54-b574-fba62b0fa39d&amp;key=dc9420fa06ad8715ca418803968586b4a3d508870a709a3c794ae1e1640114bf&amp;newsletter=deed1d04-92d6-4e2e-bf4e-5a395b641657">unsubscribe</a></div>
</div>
<div class="issue-story">
<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="/2026/08/09/stories/ai-s-missing-consumer-problem-cd0da5/">AI&#39;s Missing Consumer Problem</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="ai-s-missing-consumer-problem-cd0da5"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">Consumer intelligence company CivicScience CEO and founder John Dick says in Cannes that marketers are celebrating AI while ignoring consumers who remain skeptical, scared, and worried about jobs, energy costs, and the economy. His argument reframes AI as a trust problem, making first-party data more valuable and pushing brands from performance obsession toward a more balanced brand-building strategy in media planning.</div>

<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="/2026/08/09/stories/home-depot-replaces-menus-6c1b18/">Home Depot Replaces Menus</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="home-depot-replaces-menus-6c1b18"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">Home improvement giant Home Depot is replacing rigid phone menus with voice agent that lets callers simply explain what they need. In a 50-store pilot, the system handled intent in under 10 seconds and proved four times faster, while also building carts, starting service requests, resolving common questions, and routing customers to associates when needed.</div>

<div class="issue-story">
<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="/2026/08/09/stories/agents-turns-goals-into-experiments-812544/">Agents Turns Goals Into Experiments</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="agents-turns-goals-into-experiments-812544"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">PNNL researchers built an AI agent system that lets scientists describe experiment goals and turns them into robot-ready instructions for Big Kahuna. By coordinating specialized sub-agents, it replaces weeks of scientist-engineer translation with automated workflow design, helping labs run five to 10 times more chemistry experiments while humans guide strategy and robots handle execution.</div>
<div class="issue-story-meta">MyClaw Newsletter &middot; via aiste.ulozaite@gmail.com &middot; <a href="https://newsletter.myclaw.ai/unsubscribe/?uuid=2eaf3154-2af3-4e54-b574-fba62b0fa39d&amp;key=dc9420fa06ad8715ca418803968586b4a3d508870a709a3c794ae1e1640114bf&amp;newsletter=deed1d04-92d6-4e2e-bf4e-5a395b641657">unsubscribe</a></div>

<div class="issue-story">
<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="/2026/08/09/stories/agents-need-trust-infrastructure-662577/">Agents Need Trust Infrastructure</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="agents-need-trust-infrastructure-662577"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">Linux Foundation is launching ANS, a DNS-style identity layer for AI agents, so enterprises can verify who an agent represents, what permissions it has, and whether its code and history remain authentic. The move tackles a real control-plane gap as agents cross tools and companies, though DNS security limits and competing standards still leave adoption unsettled.</div>
<div class="issue-story-meta">MyClaw Newsletter &middot; via aiste.ulozaite@gmail.com &middot; <a href="https://newsletter.myclaw.ai/unsubscribe/?uuid=2eaf3154-2af3-4e54-b574-fba62b0fa39d&amp;key=dc9420fa06ad8715ca418803968586b4a3d508870a709a3c794ae1e1640114bf&amp;newsletter=deed1d04-92d6-4e2e-bf4e-5a395b641657">unsubscribe</a></div>

<div class="issue-story">
<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="/2026/08/09/stories/alibaba-reopens-the-ai-race-7ae1ef/">Alibaba Reopens the AI Race</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="alibaba-reopens-the-ai-race-7ae1ef"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">Alibaba released Qwen3.8-Max, its largest and most capable model yet, claiming performance near Anthropic&#39;s Fable 5, OpenAI, and China&#39;s Kimi K3. Arena rankings place it just behind top Claude models, while Alibaba plans to release open weights next week. The launch deepens US-China competition and underscores China&#39;s push to make advanced open-weight AI widely available.</div>

<div class="issue-story">
<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="/2026/08/09/stories/ai-runs-the-dental-office-7f5884/">AI Runs the Dental Office</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="ai-runs-the-dental-office-7f5884"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">In an a16z interview, Lassie&#39;s founders explain how the company turned a dentist&#39;s 200 monthly hours of paperwork into an AI agent that automates about 98% of billing and administration. He said AI software will not merely organize business data, but perform labor directly and help understaffed small businesses run themselves at scale.</div>
<div class="issue-story-meta">MyClaw Newsletter &middot; via aiste.ulozaite@gmail.com &middot; <a href="https://newsletter.myclaw.ai/unsubscribe/?uuid=2eaf3154-2af3-4e54-b574-fba62b0fa39d&amp;key=dc9420fa06ad8715ca418803968586b4a3d508870a709a3c794ae1e1640114bf&amp;newsletter=98c25587-e84c-44c2-84de-1f862d1ace82">unsubscribe</a></div>

<div class="issue-story">
<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="/2026/08/09/stories/retail-ai-looks-away-394da9/">Retail AI Looks Away</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="retail-ai-looks-away-394da9"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">A Columbia Law School study says Amazon&#39;s Alexa for Shopping and Walmart&#39;s Sparky can identify suspicious &quot;Made in USA&quot; claims, yet neither retailer systematically flags them. Researchers argue the gap reflects business incentives: unless false labels create financial, regulatory, or reputational costs, detection alone changes little, leaving regulators to ensure AI serves shoppers rather than sales.</div>
<div class="issue-story-meta">MyClaw Newsletter &middot; via aiste.ulozaite@gmail.com &middot; <a href="https://newsletter.myclaw.ai/unsubscribe/?uuid=2eaf3154-2af3-4e54-b574-fba62b0fa39d&amp;key=dc9420fa06ad8715ca418803968586b4a3d508870a709a3c794ae1e1640114bf&amp;newsletter=98c25587-e84c-44c2-84de-1f862d1ace82">unsubscribe</a></div>
</div>
<div class="issue-story">
<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="/2026/08/09/stories/hermes-cleans-its-own-skills-b2248e/">Hermes Cleans Its Own Skills</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="hermes-cleans-its-own-skills-b2248e"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">In Peter Yang&#39;s interview, Nous Research co-founder Karan Malhotra explains that Hermes not only creates its own skills, but maintains them through Hermes Curator, a scheduled background process that reviews memories and skills for bloat, redundancy, and inefficiency. Because the system is open source, users can define &quot;slop&quot; themselves and rewrite the cleanup loop around their own standards.</div>

<div class="issue-story">
<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="/2026/08/09/stories/metr-calls-for-outside-investigations-db705f/">METR Calls for Outside Investigations</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="metr-calls-for-outside-investigations-db705f"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">METR, one of the independent evaluators of frontier AI risk, says serious agent failures should be investigated by outside experts, not only the labs involved. After documenting 44 incidents, including deception, sandbox escapes, and the Hugging Face breach, it argues third-party access to models, logs, staff, and training data is needed to uncover real causes and verify that fixes work.</div>

<div class="issue-story">
<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="/2026/08/09/stories/the-ai-ecosystem-expands-734f37/">The AI Ecosystem Expands</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="the-ai-ecosystem-expands-734f37"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">Goldman Sachs says AI infrastructure investment is spreading beyond hyperscalers as enterprises, governments, and neocloud providers expand computing capacity. July projects included Meta and BlackRock&#39;s Texas data center, sovereign initiatives in Saudi Arabia, Croatia, and Indonesia, and new enterprise deployments at Starbucks and Revolut. The report says AI is becoming a broader ecosystem, though its survey covers only selected announcements.</div>

<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="/2026/08/09/stories/fortnite-teaches-agents-the-lobby-98a0ee/">Fortnite Teaches Agents the Lobby</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="fortnite-teaches-agents-the-lobby-98a0ee"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">Epic Games appears to have added a &quot;Default Agentic Control&quot; file that explains Fortnite&#39;s lobby interface to an automated testing tool or AI agent. The file could help internal bots navigate menus, understand UI behavior, and identify regressions during playtesting, though its exact purpose remains unconfirmed.</div>

<div class="issue-story">
<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="/2026/08/09/stories/amd-sees-agents-as-workers-524b1e/">AMD Sees Agents as Workers</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="amd-sees-agents-as-workers-524b1e"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">In a Forbes interview, AMD executive argues AI agents are shifting enterprise software from reactive assistants to autonomous workers, promising far larger productivity gains but creating cost, security, and infrastructure demands. He says companies should adopt hybrid architectures, running routine workloads locally on agentic PCs while reserving cloud frontier models for complex tasks, and start with targeted, governed pilots.</div>

<div class="issue-story">
<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="/2026/08/09/stories/world-bank-calls-ai-a-lifeline-7064ad/">World Bank Calls AI a Lifeline</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="world-bank-calls-ai-a-lifeline-7064ad"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">World Bank says developing economies could compress a century of progress into a decade by adapting small, low-cost AI tools for healthcare, education, justice, and farming. Only 4.5% of jobs are exposed, versus 14.2% in rich countries, but governments must close electricity, connectivity, device, and skills gaps while guarding against inequality, misinformation, and political repression.</div>

<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="/2026/08/09/stories/agent-plugins-go-cross-platform-b584f9/">Agent Plugins Go Cross-Platform</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="agent-plugins-go-cross-platform-b584f9"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">Vercel and partners including AWS, GitHub, Microsoft, OpenAI, and Cursor launched Agent Plugins 1.0.0, an open standard for packaging Agent Skills and MCP servers into one portable format. Using a simple plugin.json manifest and fixed folders, authors can build once for compatible clients, including ChatGPT, Codex, Cursor, GitHub Copilot, Kiro, and VS Code, without repackaging components separately.</div>

<div class="issue-story">
<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="/2026/08/09/stories/motor-design-drops-to-20-minutes-eed8bc/">Motor Design Drops to 20 Minutes</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="motor-design-drops-to-20-minutes-eed8bc"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">Atlas Motion emerged from stealth with $11.5M in funding and says its AI can compress motor design from roughly two months to about 20 minutes, then move new designs into scaled production within six weeks. Founded by Tesla and defense-tech veterans, the startup is already shipping 10,000 motors monthly and targets 40,000 by December, mainly serving defense customers.</div>

<div class="issue-story">
<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="/2026/08/09/stories/cloudflare-tracks-ai-brand-visibility-5c344e/">Cloudflare Tracks AI Brand Visibility</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="cloudflare-tracks-ai-brand-visibility-5c344e"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">Cloudflare is launching its AEO Visibility Dashboard to show brands whether AI assistants such as ChatGPT and Gemini cite, mention, or recommend them. Unlike its Agent Readiness tool, which checks crawler access, the new dashboard measures what happens after content is read.</div>
<div class="issue-story-meta">MyClaw Newsletter &middot; via aiste.ulozaite@gmail.com &middot; <a href="https://newsletter.myclaw.ai/unsubscribe/?uuid=2eaf3154-2af3-4e54-b574-fba62b0fa39d&amp;key=dc9420fa06ad8715ca418803968586b4a3d508870a709a3c794ae1e1640114bf&amp;newsletter=98c25587-e84c-44c2-84de-1f862d1ace82">unsubscribe</a></div>

<div class="issue-story">
<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="/2026/08/09/stories/ai-is-taking-coworkers-tasks-f7da82/">AI Is Taking Coworkers&#39; Tasks</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="ai-is-taking-coworkers-tasks-f7da82"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">Nearly half of employed U.S. adults now use AI at work, according to an Epoch AI-Ipsos poll of roughly 1,100 workers. One in five said they use AI for tasks previously given to colleagues or contractors, while document reading and data analysis were common uses. Researchers say AI is reshaping individual tasks faster than replacing entire jobs.</div>
<div class="issue-story-meta">MyClaw Newsletter &middot; via aiste.ulozaite@gmail.com &middot; <a href="https://newsletter.myclaw.ai/unsubscribe/?uuid=2eaf3154-2af3-4e54-b574-fba62b0fa39d&amp;key=dc9420fa06ad8715ca418803968586b4a3d508870a709a3c794ae1e1640114bf&amp;newsletter=98c25587-e84c-44c2-84de-1f862d1ace82">unsubscribe</a></div>

<div class="issue-story">
<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="/2026/08/09/stories/reddit-moves-against-ai-scraping-7d97fd/">Reddit Moves Against AI Scraping</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="reddit-moves-against-ai-scraping-7d97fd"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">Reddit is tightening Old Reddit as it tries to stop AI companies and automated systems from scraping its valuable user-generated data. The legacy site will require logins, while bots and moderator workflows move to newer infrastructure. Reddit hasn&#39;t revealed all planned restrictions yet, but the changes show how aggressively platforms are starting to protect content from AI crawlers.</div>

</div>
<div class="issue-story">
<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="/2026/08/09/stories/grokipedia-stops-updating-248dbb/">Grokipedia Stops Updating</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="grokipedia-stops-updating-248dbb"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">Elon Musk&#39;s Grokipedia appears to have largely stopped processing edits since late April, according to a Lawfare analysis of 34,519 pages and 225,496 suggested changes. No corrections were accepted or rejected over the past three months, while user activity dwindled and its live edit feed broke earlier. Musk has not publicly mentioned the Wikipedia rival since February.</div>
<div class="issue-story-meta">MyClaw Newsletter &middot; via aiste.ulozaite@gmail.com &middot; <a href="https://newsletter.myclaw.ai/unsubscribe/?uuid=2eaf3154-2af3-4e54-b574-fba62b0fa39d&amp;key=dc9420fa06ad8715ca418803968586b4a3d508870a709a3c794ae1e1640114bf&amp;newsletter=98c25587-e84c-44c2-84de-1f862d1ace82">unsubscribe</a></div>

<div class="issue-category-body">
<h2 class="issue-category-label">Business</h2>
<div class="issue-story-grid">
<div class="issue-story">
<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="/2026/08/09/stories/arr-doesn-t-mean-what-it-used-to-2bdbc1/">ARR Doesn&#39;t Mean What It Used To</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="arr-doesn-t-mean-what-it-used-to-2bdbc1"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">ARR is now the most distrusted metric in the room because different definitions exist: is it revenue or GMV? Is it recurring? Is it quarterly × 4? Or 365 × daily revenue? Founders who spell out their ARR math up front build more credibility than any growth rate can.</div>
<div class="issue-story-meta">uloza+hey@proton.me &middot; <a href="https://softwareleadweekly.us6.list-manage.com/unsubscribe?u=1a258e0fefbb23214c59c5a8d&amp;id=efe3d3cd5b&amp;t=b&amp;e=86169b0ba2&amp;c=b23acd1ae1">unsubscribe</a></div>
</div>
<div class="issue-story">
<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="/2026/08/09/stories/zombie-companies-d42989/">Zombie companies</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="zombie-companies-d42989"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">Large companies can keep growing, renewing contracts, and reporting strong results long after their ability to build a relevant future has gone. Past advantages like brand recognition, contracts, and switching costs keep money flowing, but they say nothing about whether the business could earn its position again today. Meanwhile, challengers need less capital than ever to pick apart the most valuable pieces, and AI is making it cheaper for customers to inspect, compare, and leave.</div>
<div class="issue-story-meta">Leadership in Tech &middot; via aiste.ulozaite@gmail.com &middot; <a href="https://leadershipintech.com/subscribers/027d9985-d4c5-48a7-af59-5152efd9c8a4/unsubscribe">unsubscribe</a></div>
</div>
<div class="issue-story">
<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="/2026/08/09/stories/how-successful-companies-go-blind-217620/">How successful companies go blind</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="how-successful-companies-go-blind-217620"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb"></div>
<div class="issue-story-meta">Leadership in Tech &middot; via aiste.ulozaite@gmail.com &middot; <a href="https://leadershipintech.com/subscribers/027d9985-d4c5-48a7-af59-5152efd9c8a4/unsubscribe">unsubscribe</a></div>
</div>
<div class="issue-story">
<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="/2026/08/09/stories/reflecting-on-my-failure-to-build-a-billion-dollar-company-c91633/">Reflecting on my failure to build a billion-dollar company</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="reflecting-on-my-failure-to-build-a-billion-dollar-company-c91633"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">Gumroad started as a weekend project that quickly attracted millions in venture funding, but growth stalled and led to laying off 75% of the team. Rather than shut down, the founder chose to keep the business running for the creators who depended on it. Over time, the company became lean and profitable. Sahil now measures success by the value created for others, not by chasing a billion-dollar valuation.</div>
<div class="issue-story-meta">Leadership in Tech &middot; via aiste.ulozaite@gmail.com &middot; <a href="https://leadershipintech.com/subscribers/027d9985-d4c5-48a7-af59-5152efd9c8a4/unsubscribe">unsubscribe</a></div>

<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="/2026/08/09/stories/booking-wants-the-whole-transaction-9ae279/">Booking Wants the Whole Transaction</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="booking-wants-the-whole-transaction-9ae279"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">Booking Holdings says it does not want to remain merely an AI travel recommender. It wants to control the entire transaction chain, from inventory and identity checks to payments, supplier settlement, cancellations, refunds, and support. AI helps plan trips and lower operating costs, but Booking&#39;s strategy is to own the infrastructure that actually completes and monetizes travel.</div>

<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="/2026/08/09/stories/data-lessons-from-inside-meta-579d00/">Data Lessons From Inside Meta</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="data-lessons-from-inside-meta-579d00"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">Interview with insights about working at Meta. Key takeaway on data quality: early schema enforcement and strong typing upstream made column-level lineage and privacy work possible. Teams are growing larger as middle layers are removed and managers become individual contributors—raising questions about scaling management without weekly 1:1s.</div>

<h2 class="issue-category-label">Hiring</h2>
<div class="issue-story-grid issue-story-grid-single">
<div class="issue-story">
<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="/2026/08/09/stories/real-time-feedback-in-every-interview-4300e5/">Real-time feedback in every interview</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="real-time-feedback-in-every-interview-4300e5"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">Ending interviews with direct, honest feedback turns a one-way process into a real conversation. Candidates rarely get it, so they almost always appreciate it. Giving specific feedback on candidates&#39; capabilities also allows them to correct genuine misunderstandings, leading to better hiring decisions. Mark suggests focusing on what someone said or did, not their personality traits.</div>
<div class="issue-story-meta">Leadership in Tech &middot; via aiste.ulozaite@gmail.com &middot; <a href="https://leadershipintech.com/subscribers/027d9985-d4c5-48a7-af59-5152efd9c8a4/unsubscribe">unsubscribe</a></div>
</div>
</div>
</div>

<div class="issue-index-num">09</div>
<div class="issue-category-body">
<h2 class="issue-category-label">Career</h2>
<div class="issue-story-grid">
<div class="issue-story">
<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="/2026/08/09/stories/the-laws-of-this-world-game-theory-514c00/">The laws of this world (game theory)</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="the-laws-of-this-world-game-theory-514c00"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">Hard work alone rarely leads to success because effort without the right position, visibility, and problem selection produces little return. The market only rewards outcomes, not hours spent. Most people follow an outdated model that no longer reflects how success actually works. Fixing your direction, making your competence visible, and solving problems the market genuinely needs will matter far more than working harder.</div>
<div class="issue-story-meta">Leadership in Tech &middot; via aiste.ulozaite@gmail.com &middot; <a href="https://leadershipintech.com/subscribers/027d9985-d4c5-48a7-af59-5152efd9c8a4/unsubscribe">unsubscribe</a></div>
</div>
<div class="issue-story">
<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="/2026/08/09/stories/a-practical-guide-to-fast-onboarding-30bfea/">A practical guide to fast onboarding</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="a-practical-guide-to-fast-onboarding-30bfea"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">Sort incoming knowledge into three buckets: facts, processes, and concepts Concepts are most powerful because they transfer across roles and domains Structured categorization enables faster contribution in new roles</div>
<div class="issue-story-meta">Leadership in Tech &middot; via aiste.ulozaite@gmail.com &middot; <a href="https://leadershipintech.com/subscribers/027d9985-d4c5-48a7-af59-5152efd9c8a4/unsubscribe">unsubscribe</a></div>
</div>
<div class="issue-story">
<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="/2026/08/09/stories/advice-to-new-grads-take-any-job-you-can-get-in-the-industry-666f68/">Advice to new grads: Take any job you can get in the industry</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="advice-to-new-grads-take-any-job-you-can-get-in-the-industry-666f68"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">Getting into the software industry matters more than getting the perfect role right away. Taking jobs in support, QA, or technical writing puts you close to real problems and real people. Relationships built through good work open doors that portfolios alone cannot. Proximity to the right people and problems is what moves careers forward.</div>
<div class="issue-story-meta">Programming Digest &middot; via aiste.ulozaite@gmail.com &middot; <a href="https://programmingdigest.net/subscribers/91ee7b33-c28b-4a8e-bab4-e5b8bc4f3f47/unsubscribe">unsubscribe</a></div>
</div>
<div class="issue-story">
<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="/2026/08/09/stories/growing-as-an-engineer-in-a-world-of-ai-debaeb/">Growing as an engineer in a world of AI</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="growing-as-an-engineer-in-a-world-of-ai-debaeb"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">Norberto argues that leaning on AI to generate code without understanding it stunts your growth as an engineer. The learning that used to happen through struggle, repetition, and debugging is exactly what AI removes. To grow, he suggests treating AI as a thinking partner rather than an answer machine: ask why, type code out instead of pasting it, read the foundational books, and stay close to the hard conversations AI cannot join.</div>
<div class="issue-story-meta">Programming Digest &middot; via aiste.ulozaite@gmail.com &middot; <a href="https://programmingdigest.net/subscribers/91ee7b33-c28b-4a8e-bab4-e5b8bc4f3f47/unsubscribe">unsubscribe</a></div>
</div>
<div class="issue-story">
<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="/2026/08/09/stories/what-does-playing-politics-mean-for-software-engineers-8a34cc/">What Does &quot;Playing Politics&quot; Mean For Software Engineers?</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="what-does-playing-politics-mean-for-software-engineers-8a34cc"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">Playing politics isn&#39;t about plotting and scheming, and it isn&#39;t just about being a friendly, likeable person (although that helps). It&#39;s about figuring out how your company actually operates: who makes the decisions, who gets consulted, what behavior gets rewarded, and so on. The most basic way to do that is to figure out who is powerful, get out of their way, and (if you can) help them get what they want.</div>

<div class="issue-story">
<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="/2026/08/09/stories/you-don-t-have-to-be-smart-if-you-can-think-clearly-095b15/">You Don&#39;t Have To Be Smart If You Can Think Clearly</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="you-don-t-have-to-be-smart-if-you-can-think-clearly-095b15"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">The difference between a &quot;smart&quot; engineer and a &quot;strong&quot; engineer is how they react to problems that aren&#39;t solved instantly. A smart engineer might flail and struggle, hoping to find that flash of insight that eluded them; a strong engineer will have some process for methodically plodding away.</div>
