<div class="issue-category-body">
<h2 class="issue-category-label">Project Management</h2>
<div class="issue-story-grid issue-story-grid-single">
<div class="issue-story">
<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="/2026/08/09/stories/laws-of-project-management-79a28b/">Laws of project management</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="laws-of-project-management-79a28b"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">Lucas presents ten practical laws for managing projects successfully. He explains that projects succeed by solving real problems, adapting to new information, and delivering working solutions early. The laws emphasize flexible planning, clear priorities, teamwork, operational readiness, and testing important assumptions first. Together, they provide simple guidance for building products that meet user needs while balancing business goals, time, scope, and quality.</div>

<div class="issue-story-blurb">A fake recruiter on LinkedIn sent Appaji a Python developer job offer with a take-home coding test hidden inside a zip file. The project looked clean at first, but running a simple directory listing revealed pre-loaded Git hooks designed to silently download and execute malicious scripts the moment any Git command ran. The scripts then installed Node.js in the background and ran obfuscated code targeting crypto wallets, with unique tracking IDs assigned to each victim.</div>

<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="/2026/08/09/stories/how-to-write-an-effective-software-design-document-b1ef66/">How to write an effective software design document</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="how-to-write-an-effective-software-design-document-b1ef66"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">Writing a design doc before coding saves time by forcing clear thinking and helping teams coordinate. It should focus on high-stakes decisions where getting things wrong is costly, not minor choices that are easy to fix later. Key sections include goals, background, diagrams, security, and open issues. The right length depends on project complexity, team size, and risk.</div>
<div class="issue-story-meta">Leadership in Tech &middot; via aiste.ulozaite@gmail.com &middot; <a href="https://leadershipintech.com/subscribers/027d9985-d4c5-48a7-af59-5152efd9c8a4/unsubscribe">unsubscribe</a></div>
</div>
<div class="issue-story">
<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="/2026/08/09/stories/perfection-is-not-over-engineering-695af8/">Perfection is not over-engineering</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="perfection-is-not-over-engineering-695af8"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">Over-engineering means solving the wrong problem, not caring too much about quality. A perfect solution exists when requirements are clear enough that only one answer fits. The real culprit behind bloated systems is poor requirements gathering, not ambition. Get every constraint on the table and the right solution becomes obvious.</div>
<div class="issue-story-meta">Leadership in Tech &middot; via aiste.ulozaite@gmail.com &middot; <a href="https://leadershipintech.com/subscribers/027d9985-d4c5-48a7-af59-5152efd9c8a4/unsubscribe">unsubscribe</a></div>
</div>
<div class="issue-story">
<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="/2026/08/09/stories/how-i-find-problems-to-solve-as-a-staff-engineer-3866cc/">How I find problems to solve as a staff engineer</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="how-i-find-problems-to-solve-as-a-staff-engineer-3866cc"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">Finding good problems to work on comes from paying close attention to what people struggle with day to day, not from scheduled thinking sessions. Letting issues accumulate over time reveals patterns: separate complaints often share a common root cause. Testing ideas through prototypes and conversations helps confirm whether a solution is real or just elegant on paper. Over time, solving the right problems builds trust and opens doors to shaping what a team builds next.</div>
<div class="issue-story-meta">Leadership in Tech &middot; via aiste.ulozaite@gmail.com &middot; <a href="https://leadershipintech.com/subscribers/027d9985-d4c5-48a7-af59-5152efd9c8a4/unsubscribe">unsubscribe</a></div>
</div>
<div class="issue-story">
<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="/2026/08/09/stories/how-to-measure-engineering-c2f941/">How to measure engineering</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="how-to-measure-engineering-c2f941"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">ValueSum metric tracks value delivered over time by scoring shipped items Works across product, engineering, and design teams Enables learning by rescoring features months later to validate predictions</div>
<div class="issue-story-meta">Leadership in Tech &middot; via aiste.ulozaite@gmail.com &middot; <a href="https://leadershipintech.com/subscribers/027d9985-d4c5-48a7-af59-5152efd9c8a4/unsubscribe">unsubscribe</a></div>
</div>
<div class="issue-story">
<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="/2026/08/09/stories/beyond-happy-path-engineering-time-34bd58/">Beyond happy path engineering: Time</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="beyond-happy-path-engineering-time-34bd58"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">Gabor explains why time is more complicated in production systems than it first appears. He distinguishes wall-clock, monotonic, logical, and business time, showing when each should be used. He also covers clock drift, expiration, scheduled jobs, time zones, competing updates, and reconciliation. The main lesson is to treat time as a design boundary and make its assumptions explicit.</div>
<div class="issue-story-meta">Programming Digest &middot; via aiste.ulozaite@gmail.com &middot; <a href="https://programmingdigest.net/subscribers/91ee7b33-c28b-4a8e-bab4-e5b8bc4f3f47/unsubscribe">unsubscribe</a></div>
</div>

<h2 class="issue-category-label">Testing</h2>
<div class="issue-story-grid">
<div class="issue-story">
<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="https://leadershipintech.com/links/22692/027d9985-d4c5-48a7-af59-5152efd9c8a4/email">Still writing tests manually? Meticulous AI is here.</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="still-writing-tests-manually-meticulous-ai-is-here-ce47fd"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">Most teams are forced to make the tradeoff between better coverage and more maintenance effort. But top engineering teams like Notion, Dropbox, Wealthsimple and LaunchDarkly have discovered a new testing paradigm. Built by ex-Palantir engineers, Meticulous creates and maintains E2E UI tests that covers every edge case of your web app without any developer effort - making it the only tool to improve both product quality and dev velocity.</div>
<div class="issue-story-meta">Leadership in Tech &middot; Sponsored &middot; via aiste.ulozaite@gmail.com &middot; <a href="https://leadershipintech.com/subscribers/027d9985-d4c5-48a7-af59-5152efd9c8a4/unsubscribe">unsubscribe</a></div>
</div>
<div class="issue-story">
<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="https://link.mail.beehiiv.com/ss/c/u001.NxDti-hLnxnUue0yjhAbR9ihDugbmr0sv--A_jFIRE3EayKXEu62IIdTGAjdYDuNoONyUgwxz5LlNC1hhF1KaH1TGAVJUk8lfCpAXJOkHqpPzye6KAGMdLe_UVrfzve8dfhhOsJhxnjs1x3sSbnqOXrCyEL0S9ETG8IW-fxNYxuN9srHRbQlBxrjoTRASu14OZ8qaYvw6s0joCSwXqCwxQ/4sl/h7_ivzkxSm-YefoUD5CPxQ/h14/h001.J56ZXSgwo2a2fBW64fh5ppm0jBSqO8vCJYtnFsbhNYs">Prefactoring: Clear The Way For Your New Feature</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="prefactoring-clear-the-way-for-your-new-feature-651232"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">Prefactoring (short for &quot;preparatory refactoring&quot;) is the practice of reworking existing code to make it more suitable for an upcoming change before you actually implement the new functionality. Instead of cleaning up code as an afterthought or trying to force a new feature into an incompatible structure, you restructure the codebase first.</div>

</div>
<div class="issue-story">
<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="https://link.mail.beehiiv.com/ss/c/u001.3a5P_SwQzY5x8USD2q4p0m895QuVtqvkW11dSkgIg8I7yY6GUJWSXHFbDSHBQ6GNO97jmxS8vjQnwyyz4O2h9BR-DYNcxQc37-aKgDZlUstD0r3LTm4e0KFs3UMbZO0oT_ZRwffOYUv7GO1JJAoaJKP74yn-GMBaipXL1l8B_mP-trBWG_d-oD-jEMyyxo0sMmLOlJnZY45RskdLcKnO7WgTwO-MBlaHtWuWUfnpCVT-M-7JcY4akkqPGlK6et_YWNWZz0Jm_KQnMgaLX4OSCN1GyaCXBNsUPtZC-o0P1z-5wOtTKAJnj_RZ3DIAiVEg/4sw/eBYeDbHiRy6heWHd_5s8-A/h12/h001.b0CEs04LFaMF-iw8E9tza6cSOu3AQCS-JMplxXBqfEk">How Miro Builds And Tests Agentic Features At Scale</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="how-miro-builds-and-tests-agentic-features-at-scale-d01a46"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">Miro&#39;s AI team was building a new AI architecture across nearly 250 microservices, and one shared environment meant tests constantly collided. With Signadot, they moved to verifying changes in parallel with lightweight environments in the cluster. Agents drive full end-to-end tests against live services and iterate before a PR opens. Engineers get verified working code without waiting for slots.</div>

<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="/2026/08/09/stories/the-economic-benefit-of-refactoring-bcd304/">The economic benefit of refactoring</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="the-economic-benefit-of-refactoring-bcd304"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">An AI agent built a 150,000-line application, but its data access layer ballooned into a single 17,000-line file with massive repetition. To test whether refactoring helps AI agents work more efficiently, the same code change was measured before and after each refactoring step. After 15 steps, the tokens needed to make that change dropped by 83%, because the agent could read fewer, better-organized files instead of scanning one giant one.</div>

<div class="issue-story-blurb">Reversible technical decisions become costly to undo as systems accumulate dependencies Optionality requires active maintenance and intentional design Suggests documenting assumptions, estimating migration costs, and testing exit plans</div>

<div class="issue-story">
<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="/2026/08/09/stories/how-big-is-a-git-commit-d2d2e9/">How big is a Git commit?</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="how-big-is-a-git-commit-d2d2e9"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">Git stores data as compressed objects, so commit sizes vary based on how compressible your files are. Small commits add a few kilobytes of overhead, while larger text files can compress down to around 10% of their original size. Git also packs objects together over time to save even more space, making it very storage efficient overall.</div>
<div class="issue-story-meta">Programming Digest &middot; via aiste.ulozaite@gmail.com &middot; <a href="https://programmingdigest.net/subscribers/91ee7b33-c28b-4a8e-bab4-e5b8bc4f3f47/unsubscribe">unsubscribe</a></div>
</div>
<div class="issue-story">
<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="/2026/08/09/stories/meet-alice-alice-is-impatient-2cfeef/">Meet alice. Alice is impatient.</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="meet-alice-alice-is-impatient-2cfeef"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">Users and operators often measure service performance very differently. Operators count each outage or slow request once, while users experience long outages repeatedly, making them feel much worse. This is called the inspection paradox: users see a time-weighted version of your metrics, so a one-minute average recovery time can feel like an hour to them. Long tail events matter far more to users than standard averages suggest.</div>
<div class="issue-story-meta">Programming Digest &middot; via aiste.ulozaite@gmail.com &middot; <a href="https://programmingdigest.net/subscribers/91ee7b33-c28b-4a8e-bab4-e5b8bc4f3f47/unsubscribe">unsubscribe</a></div>
</div>

<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="/2026/08/09/stories/a-peek-into-reddit-s-anti-spam-internals-25ae89/">A peek into reddit&#39;s anti-spam internals</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="a-peek-into-reddit-s-anti-spam-internals-25ae89"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">A bug in Reddit&#39;s spam filter briefly exposed internal removal reasons to a moderator in 2021. The leaked messages revealed several tools Reddit uses behind the scenes, including Google&#39;s Perspective API for spam scoring, a rules engine called Spamurai, domain bans, and even URL inspection that scans linked pages for known spam patterns.</div>
<div class="issue-story-meta">Programming Digest &middot; via aiste.ulozaite@gmail.com &middot; <a href="https://programmingdigest.net/subscribers/91ee7b33-c28b-4a8e-bab4-e5b8bc4f3f47/unsubscribe">unsubscribe</a></div>

Protector offers on-demand security details</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="who-needs-an-uber-for-bodyguards-protector-offers-on-demand--fcd490"></span></h3>

<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="/2026/08/09/stories/planned-amazon-data-center-could-become-the-biggest-climate--963e8f/">Planned Amazon data center could become the biggest climate polluter in the U.S.</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="planned-amazon-data-center-could-become-the-biggest-climate--963e8f"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">As part of a planned Texas data center, Amazon is investing in an on-site power plant that could reportedly become the largest source of climate pollution in the United States.</div>
<div class="issue-story-meta">aiste.ulozaite@gmail.com &middot; <a href="https://link.techcrunch.com/oc/6871770a273e032f335072f3ry6g4.63s/6d0246ee">unsubscribe</a></div>
</div>
<div class="issue-story">
<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="https://link.mail.beehiiv.com/ss/c/u001.u7AEjFG7xD7PeXgObD5uI68iIspGEhbpwFIn8knMoQ8LGD40-fzeETOxjykaYOltg3egxWlCXD0DP31xABDu-zjVhZVS4DeA035c4tNpjr_5F4qlQEn2ARI3QrU7hNlj9CFPSnD0j2ozZQGsRfC_zHEmL3kFEMyyhU-hd1NjmczyMAxWuTHovHYNTcbFNe8SMER3ogrgI3pAWjHctAAhqA9W_04C4gVuKICz9XMIf6Y/4ss/FlauVPTqTeGKDEKpox9WIg/h12/h001.4EX88M190WjRJnK4MVeO8J4-wIfAgA-mspwDk3ks">The Economic Benefit Of Refactoring</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="the-economic-benefit-of-refactoring-76fb1d"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">The goal of refactoring an agentic code base is to spend tokens now in refactoring to make token consumption for future work lower. An experiment should be able to show that as this file was refactored the token cost of making separate feature implementations in this code base would decrease.</div>

<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="https://link.mail.beehiiv.com/ss/c/u001.PFjHD-ii0vvUj1FcNnOS5r8FWr9qP27WB7EkR0vdkxj495K0yi5xjiR9pdEZx1LA30waIHHGF89JhpLXxzqfaI8_rfnXufLwoLmPWBeXymhgXi49JAkuRak2GkB_W5XFjpGS4-FJD5C95tnm0oeshzIEzxEuz5HCBsM8rUdB7DSi98REg9bLvSk_NbhamH5pfVOTqXVUFHFkov3nJKz5nA/4ss/FlauVPTqTeGKDEKpox9WIg/h16/h001.NJJODsSmuXawdSMaZQhV4airSq3bTCuKHwJw8eGPrhA">How&#39;s Linear So Fast? A Technical Breakdown</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="how-s-linear-so-fast-a-technical-breakdown-e7d409"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">A few milliseconds is all it takes to update an issue in Linear. A traditional CRUD app doing the same thing takes about 300ms. How do they do it? There&#39;s no secret silver bullet to performance. The reality is that it&#39;s built from the ground up on the right foundation, then improved by countless decisions. My goal is to walk through some of the techniques that make Linear feel the way it does and help you implement the same.</div>

<div class="issue-story">
<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="/2026/08/09/stories/microsoft-softens-its-ai-bill-d41d5a/">Microsoft Softens Its AI Bill</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="microsoft-softens-its-ai-bill-d41d5a"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">To soften the reported cost of its AI buildout, Microsoft is extending the estimated life of data centers and offices from 15 to 25 years, shifting more leases outside reported capex. The accounting change does not reduce actual investment: 2026 spending remains about $175 billion, while fiscal 2027 capex is still expected to rise further.</div>
<div class="issue-story-meta">MyClaw Newsletter &middot; via aiste.ulozaite@gmail.com &middot; <a href="https://newsletter.myclaw.ai/unsubscribe/?uuid=2eaf3154-2af3-4e54-b574-fba62b0fa39d&amp;key=dc9420fa06ad8715ca418803968586b4a3d508870a709a3c794ae1e1640114bf&amp;newsletter=98c25587-e84c-44c2-84de-1f862d1ace82">unsubscribe</a></div>

<div class="issue-category-body">
<h2 class="issue-category-label">Operations</h2>
<div class="issue-story-grid">
<div class="issue-story">
<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="/2026/08/09/stories/code-yellow-code-red-f66f4b/">Code yellow, code red</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="code-yellow-code-red-f66f4b"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">James shares his experience of how repeated outages were eroding customer trust, leading to a formal company-wide Code Yellow, targeting eight consecutive weeks of zero downtime. The effort involved auditing the entire stack, fixing rate limiting, adding circuit breakers, resolving memory leaks, and overhauling incident processes. The target was hit. Code Yellows work best with a clear problem statement, measurable exit criteria, full organisational transparency, and genuine authority to reprioritise work across teams.</div>
<div class="issue-story-meta">Leadership in Tech &middot; via aiste.ulozaite@gmail.com &middot; <a href="https://leadershipintech.com/subscribers/027d9985-d4c5-48a7-af59-5152efd9c8a4/unsubscribe">unsubscribe</a></div>
</div>
<div class="issue-story">
<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="/2026/08/09/stories/notes-on-incidents-331e2e/">Notes on incidents</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="notes-on-incidents-331e2e"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">Most incidents resolve without any action, and rushing to fix things often makes them worse. The best first step is to do nothing, take a breath, and observe. Simple fixes like disabling a feature flag usually solve the problem, but only if you know the system well enough to act decisively. Fixing incidents earns goodwill, but it is not a reliable path to lasting influence.</div>
<div class="issue-story-meta">Programming Digest &middot; via aiste.ulozaite@gmail.com &middot; <a href="https://programmingdigest.net/subscribers/91ee7b33-c28b-4a8e-bab4-e5b8bc4f3f47/unsubscribe">unsubscribe</a></div>
</div>
</div>
</div>
<div class="issue-category-rule"></div>
<div class="issue-index-num">17</div>
<div class="issue-category-body">
<h2 class="issue-category-label">Decision-Making</h2>
<div class="issue-story-grid issue-story-grid-single">
<div class="issue-story">
<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="/2026/08/09/stories/the-37-rule-3ad93b/">The 37% rule</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="the-37-rule-3ad93b"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">There is a mathematical strategy to help make better decisions when choosing from a sequence of options you cannot revisit. It works by spending the first 37% of options on observation only, then picking the next option that beats everything seen so far. This gives you roughly a 1 in 3 chance of finding the best option. The key lesson is simple: calibrate long enough to set a benchmark, but not so long that you miss your best choice.</div>
<div class="issue-story-meta">Leadership in Tech &middot; via aiste.ulozaite@gmail.com &middot; <a href="https://leadershipintech.com/subscribers/027d9985-d4c5-48a7-af59-5152efd9c8a4/unsubscribe">unsubscribe</a></div>
</div>
</div>
</div>

<div class="issue-index-num">18</div>
<div class="issue-category-body">
<h2 class="issue-category-label">Analytics</h2>
<div class="issue-story-grid issue-story-grid-single">
<div class="issue-story">
<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="https://leadershipintech.com/links/22842/027d9985-d4c5-48a7-af59-5152efd9c8a4/email">How much of your traffic is actually AI bots? Most teams are guessing low</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="how-much-of-your-traffic-is-actually-ai-bots-most-teams-are--641812"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">Industry benchmarks put automated traffic at over half of all web activity, yet 82% of enterprises estimate AI bots account for under 25% of theirs. That blind spot skews your analytics, degrades cache efficiency, and buries real signals in telemetry noise. Based on a survey of 300 enterprise leaders, this report breaks down how engineering teams are getting real-time visibility into bot traffic at scale, without blocking the automation they depend on.</div>
<div class="issue-story-meta">Leadership in Tech &middot; Sponsored &middot; via aiste.ulozaite@gmail.com &middot; <a href="https://leadershipintech.com/subscribers/027d9985-d4c5-48a7-af59-5152efd9c8a4/unsubscribe">unsubscribe</a></div>
</div>
</div>
</div>
<div class="issue-category-rule"></div>
<div class="issue-index-num">19</div>
<div class="issue-category-body">
<h2 class="issue-category-label">Metrics</h2>
<div class="issue-story-grid issue-story-grid-single">
<div class="issue-story">
<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="/2026/08/09/stories/please-don-t-smooth-the-metrics-635cc1/">Please don&#39;t smooth the metrics</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="please-don-t-smooth-the-metrics-635cc1"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">Smoothing metrics helps investors spot long-term trends, but operators should avoid it. It hides the very spikes and dips that signal real problems. A worked example shows how smoothed churn masked a sales team expanding to the wrong customers, delaying a critical board conversation by six months while the damage grew. Raw, volatile numbers tell the truth.</div>
<div class="issue-story-meta">Leadership in Tech &middot; via aiste.ulozaite@gmail.com &middot; <a href="https://leadershipintech.com/subscribers/027d9985-d4c5-48a7-af59-5152efd9c8a4/unsubscribe">unsubscribe</a></div>
</div>
</div>
</div>

<div class="issue-index-num">20</div>
<div class="issue-category-body">
<h2 class="issue-category-label">Architecture</h2>
<div class="issue-story-grid">
<div class="issue-story">
<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="https://leadershipintech.com/links/22883/027d9985-d4c5-48a7-af59-5152efd9c8a4/email">A simple view on progressive software architecture</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="a-simple-view-on-progressive-software-architecture-b31f8f"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">The way software is built is changing. Yet, architecture thinking is more important than ever. Learn about key principles and frameworks we believe are critical to thriving in this new, chaotic world.</div>
<div class="issue-story-meta">Leadership in Tech &middot; Sponsored &middot; via aiste.ulozaite@gmail.com &middot; <a href="https://leadershipintech.com/subscribers/027d9985-d4c5-48a7-af59-5152efd9c8a4/unsubscribe">unsubscribe</a></div>
</div>
<div class="issue-story">
<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="https://link.mail.beehiiv.com/ss/c/u001.Ira8ofUIq_P_lCLURPEgQ2_9_8Gutp2DJMIJWuPHbx0LFX-a7YUutz2F7JFh5kBpD0c2_QusG-UxuYOk3EX9JBhuA3weomaMPjAT4b8ikGJsZthNlD8NJXkncFEplGtMaDQwTkXhiIVBFXzQVPZYKasOYT9pcANKTncs2ZHyOIf1YgC4VNGzfCn9nSaoQk_O5mdXYq0TBKVZzdwEGDe8H4EPBrIXd0En8J24oY1JMg1hOn-BS8p5v6NlTfIoznQI/4sp/EVxvD-HVT8KkXqeJSLbjnw/h14/h001.BSiCGBd6CM7gtfvUoaeBYE_WOa3Up65CRMhkg6irJD0">Building Ask DoorDash: A Platform For Building and Evolving Agents</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="building-ask-doordash-a-platform-for-building-and-evolving-a-f9f100"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">We built Ask DoorDash on a common platform that lets domain teams build and evolve their agents without rebuilding the systems beneath them. We judged the platform by two practical outcomes: how quickly teams could add features and domains, and how quickly they could evaluate and release improvements to cost, quality, and latency.</div>

</div>
<div class="issue-story">
<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="https://link.mail.beehiiv.com/ss/c/u001.K3FzaWG5vnes9asChrWWUA8YzIx96XRnBgmCG8utQoWqerjMxrlN-55rLE-RAXv6snqi71tvzAhRLl2rs-PuSOsSrPDQwe5OQv2C_hlLYh7v9TQ1u3rVZ4_PKYxVQArlQXBi4QpTLSIS6jMzpFYHxHgimpaahGMZB4y-YR0YkzhEmPhOUzsordC30DH_n_2tDW__ldLG4KS4D7ppVRIRjA/4sw/eBYeDbHiRy6heWHd_5s8-A/h14/h001.VIxxDgc_9YNkXEMw8CTKXGgkiuC7FKqCcuApLGTHsTQ">The Bedrock of Software Design</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="the-bedrock-of-software-design-3ea57c"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">Nothing has influenced the way I design software more than algebraic data types. It sounds overly academic—the kind of term that makes people roll their eyes or scares them away altogether. But honestly, it only sounds scary. The idea itself is quite simple, and learning it showed me a fundamentally better way to build software.</div>

<h2 class="issue-category-label">Wellness</h2>
<div class="issue-story-grid issue-story-grid-single">
<div class="issue-story">
<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="/2026/08/09/stories/finding-calm-in-life-and-work-40e7b4/">Finding calm in life and work</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="finding-calm-in-life-and-work-40e7b4"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">Anxiety measurably reduces performance, pushing people to bottom third of peers Modern work culture rewards busyness over output, triggering chronic low-grade stress Simple habits like clear boundaries, movement, social connection, and meaningful work enable calm</div>
<div class="issue-story-meta">Leadership in Tech &middot; via aiste.ulozaite@gmail.com &middot; <a href="https://leadershipintech.com/subscribers/027d9985-d4c5-48a7-af59-5152efd9c8a4/unsubscribe">unsubscribe</a></div>
</div>
</div>
</div>
<div class="issue-category-rule"></div>
<div class="issue-index-num">22</div>
<div class="issue-category-body">
<h2 class="issue-category-label">Programming</h2>
<div class="issue-story-grid">
<div class="issue-story">
<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="/2026/08/09/stories/small-programming-tricks-7aeb41/">Small programming tricks</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="small-programming-tricks-7aeb41"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">Small tricks and shortcuts add up to meaningful gains in engineering productivity. Things like fuzzy shell history search, git pickaxe, or knowing a single command to spin up a local server can save real time without requiring deep background knowledge. The same applies inside companies, where knowing who to ask or where to find a doc can be just as valuable. Sharing one tip a day with your team is a simple way to spread this kind of knowledge.</div>
<div class="issue-story-meta">Programming Digest &middot; via aiste.ulozaite@gmail.com &middot; <a href="https://programmingdigest.net/subscribers/91ee7b33-c28b-4a8e-bab4-e5b8bc4f3f47/unsubscribe">unsubscribe</a></div>
</div>
<div class="issue-story">
<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="/2026/08/09/stories/running-an-obfuscated-bash-script-from-uniqlo-s-tee-2dea5e/">Running an obfuscated bash script from Uniqlo&#39;s tee</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="running-an-obfuscated-bash-script-from-uniqlo-s-tee-2dea5e"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">A Uniqlo t-shirt made in partnership with Akamai contains a real bash script printed on the back, encoded in base64. After carefully transcribing and decoding it, the script turns out to be a harmless Easter egg that displays a colorful animated &quot;Peace for All&quot; message in the terminal. Akamai designed the shirt as a nod to the early internet era, with the code serving as a reference to Linux.</div>
<div class="issue-story-meta">Programming Digest &middot; via aiste.ulozaite@gmail.com &middot; <a href="https://programmingdigest.net/subscribers/91ee7b33-c28b-4a8e-bab4-e5b8bc4f3f47/unsubscribe">unsubscribe</a></div>
</div>
<div class="issue-story">
<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="/2026/08/09/stories/the-depth-first-search-pattern-exploring-trees-and-graphs-61488f/">The depth-first search pattern: Exploring trees and graphs</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="the-depth-first-search-pattern-exploring-trees-and-graphs-61488f"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">Depth-first search is a recursion pattern for exploring trees, graphs, and matrices. It works by going as deep as possible along one path before backtracking and trying another. To avoid infinite loops in graphs, you track visited nodes and skip them on future visits. Depth-first search fits problems where you need to find paths, check reachability, or explore all possibilities through a structure.</div>
<div class="issue-story-meta">Programming Digest &middot; via aiste.ulozaite@gmail.com &middot; <a href="https://programmingdigest.net/subscribers/91ee7b33-c28b-4a8e-bab4-e5b8bc4f3f47/unsubscribe">unsubscribe</a></div>
</div>
<div class="issue-story">
<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="/2026/08/09/stories/everyone-should-know-simd-141d6a/">Everyone should know SIMD</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="everyone-should-know-simd-141d6a"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">SIMD lets a CPU process multiple values at once instead of one at a time, turning slow byte-by-byte loops into chunk-based operations that run 4x to 16x faster. Despite its reputation for complexity, common SIMD code follows the same five steps every time: broadcast constants, loop over chunks, operate on all values at once, reduce the result, then handle leftovers with a normal loop. Once you learn that pattern, writing SIMD is nearly as simple as writing a regular loop.</div>
<div class="issue-story-meta">Programming Digest &middot; via aiste.ulozaite@gmail.com &middot; <a href="https://programmingdigest.net/subscribers/91ee7b33-c28b-4a8e-bab4-e5b8bc4f3f47/unsubscribe">unsubscribe</a></div>
</div>
</div>
</div>
<div class="issue-category-rule"></div>
<div class="issue-index-num">23</div>
<div class="issue-category-body">
<h2 class="issue-category-label">Performance</h2>
<div class="issue-story-grid">
<div class="issue-story">
<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="/2026/08/09/stories/when-if-slows-you-down-avoid-it-6a76bc/">When &#39;if&#39; slows you down, avoid it</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="when-if-slows-you-down-avoid-it-6a76bc"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">Branch mispredictions slow CPUs down because they force the processor to discard queued work and start over. Writing branchless code removes unpredictable jumps and lets the CPU run at full speed. A simple filter loop rewritten to use a conditional increment instead of an if statement ran nearly 10 times faster on an Apple M1. Compilers cannot make this change automatically because they cannot safely assume unconditional memory writes are always allowed.</div>
<div class="issue-story-meta">Programming Digest &middot; via aiste.ulozaite@gmail.com &middot; <a href="https://programmingdigest.net/subscribers/91ee7b33-c28b-4a8e-bab4-e5b8bc4f3f47/unsubscribe">unsubscribe</a></div>
</div>
<div class="issue-story">
<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="/2026/08/09/stories/quadrupling-code-performance-with-a-useless-if-c96f9e/">Quadrupling Code Performance With A &quot;Useless&quot; If</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="quadrupling-code-performance-with-a-useless-if-c96f9e"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">We don&#39;t expect too many chunks, so next_j[i][j] is quite likely to just be equal to j. If we could tell the CPU to predict that j stays intact, the loop would become throughput-bound rather than latency-bound.</div>

</div>
<div class="issue-story">
<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="https://link.mail.beehiiv.com/ss/c/u001.u7AEjFG7xD7PeXgObD5uIxG0oTKnfTi2Mbt-iquwjPCmCWP6ikoOvR4kRdE6QZkUoa6oiPXl34UQgNGSw69SMfsXZlPLcbKE1jEqJc1zPWPb2Rsr_RosJBTfUW06L2oW76zog8pD8Ty38bLRChRn4UyquytZXuEtsXVu_SmL5Xzp0jrwidNCISsJlDv245n9/4sl/h7_ivzkxSm-YefoUD5CPxQ/h15/h001.NilWF4KMZLswV6daiLFQBG_xPBYGv9NmDsl6CL9wUg0">Everyone Should Know SIMD</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="everyone-should-know-simd-bbfe9c"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">SIMD has a reputation for being complex. I&#39;ve met many very good software engineers who dismiss it as something too complex to learn or a niche optimization meant for only the highest-performance software, not useful in everyday programming.</div>

</div>
<div class="issue-story">
<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="https://link.mail.beehiiv.com/ss/c/u001.TDQMwwTUsDB62J0OSnrlnP-CDVZWyHDKOJmx-kzUWBS-xot60P7Uds5WKnOvN6ebYYFt7mB3td3EPB_b9paj-X7qY4BX-PavS6Oc2HIe-IPBU4ANQQq80evRmT30HbSWvM_4GFP5iEvkv7V5HJlq1QD2fLeKCo6RS_2uWi6krl4/4sw/eBYeDbHiRy6heWHd_5s8-A/h15/h001.0m1ghaXy56ia8KRC3pUMHP1d1GF0XuBGhsfc2BgWAlo">Every Byte Matters</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="every-byte-matters-1e9c41"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">Farid argues that classic analysis isn&#39;t enough. The size of your data matters as much as your algorithm. CPUs pull memory in 64-byte &quot;cache lines,&quot; so how tightly your data packs into those lines impacts speed.</div>

<h2 class="issue-category-label">Systems</h2>
<div class="issue-story-grid issue-story-grid-single">
<div class="issue-story">
<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="/2026/08/09/stories/virtual-memory-from-first-principles-1175ba/">Virtual memory from first principles</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="virtual-memory-from-first-principles-1175ba"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">Abhinav provides a practical introduction to virtual memory, explaining its core mechanisms, including address translation, demand paging, copy-on-write, memory mapping, and page reclaim. He also explores their impact on system performance, memory management, and observability, equipping readers with a mental model for building and debugging data-intensive systems.</div>
<div class="issue-story-meta">Programming Digest &middot; via aiste.ulozaite@gmail.com &middot; <a href="https://programmingdigest.net/subscribers/91ee7b33-c28b-4a8e-bab4-e5b8bc4f3f47/unsubscribe">unsubscribe</a></div>
</div>
</div>
</div>
<div class="issue-category-rule"></div>
<div class="issue-index-num">25</div>
<div class="issue-category-body">
<h2 class="issue-category-label">Tools</h2>
<div class="issue-story-grid">
<div class="issue-story">
<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="/2026/08/09/stories/good-tools-are-invisible-c6695b/">Good tools are invisible</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="good-tools-are-invisible-c6695b"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">Good tools should be invisible. When a tool starts creating friction, that&#39;s a flaw, not a feature. Yet many people reframe their tools&#39; weaknesses as fun puzzles to solve, then mistake that feeling of cleverness for real productivity. The best sign a tool is working for you is that you stop noticing it entirely.</div>
<div class="issue-story-meta">Programming Digest &middot; via aiste.ulozaite@gmail.com &middot; <a href="https://programmingdigest.net/subscribers/91ee7b33-c28b-4a8e-bab4-e5b8bc4f3f47/unsubscribe">unsubscribe</a></div>
</div>
<div class="issue-story">
<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="https://link.mail.beehiiv.com/ss/c/u001.Ira8ofUIq_P_lCLURPEgQ8Dok6h2-kZwEtf4UdHH35ie0G9WuvSVkxf4P69MbXIar8whTXZJuVkflAI4PfIPTxjBVqO-PHWBLXFrcPX5S24l-JeilN3g96vsN4e7H-shNTOxLPhuakAdrr3sqLc73w/4sp/EVxvD-HVT8KkXqeJSLbjnw/h3/h001.djy3nlD7EJ51vT72CErE_1LPUobW16RABF_rojEKrQg">Review The Actual Change, Not The File List</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="review-the-actual-change-not-the-file-list-d1670d"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">AI writes more code than ever. Reviewing it shouldn&#39;t mean scrolling forty files in alphabetical order. CodeRabbit Review reorganizes any pull request from a flat file list into a structured, layer-by-layer walkthrough - the logical reading order of the change, not the order your platform happens to sort it. Every range gets its own plain-language summary, with sequence diagrams, state machines, and ERDs generated inline wherever a visual earns its place. Cohorts group related files and chunks so you review one idea at a time. Layers order them so foundational changes - data shapes, contracts - come before the code that depends on them. Code Peek lets you click any variable, function, class or type to see its definition and usages without leaving the tab. Semantic Diff cuts past formatting noise to show what actually changed. From the team that pioneered AI code reviews. 2M reviews every week. 6M repos. 15K customers. Free during early access.</div>
<div class="issue-story-meta">Pointer &middot; Sponsored &middot; via aiste.ulozaite@gmail.com &middot; <a href="https://link.mail.beehiiv.com/ss/c/u001.PFjHD-ii0vvUj1FcNnOS5qqF3eJEkrxympSzSDDZUB29F4951mhY03iCawzSQ6poZedL3n7H-lMzRTsKnY9bvtDfJYA8pICpvyyNYWT98Xtr9WdPcSpxxaa7Q5dr87aiCZFXsxQxqLiYcZ56a4Ef5p6WLiBwZxYfedrFF_eOOfRHMWcN_V3BWcxy6qycqBUNPEYgd7zZVK972EA-M0gdzRxoBADsrrhMTnQr0tDDFTCLi_CYV6TrK5FCv_bJGEc6hagtM5Fa3NC4mcOAZXVMWjN2iWCkvD9DOJOiHCHLdHGLuMRXh9WEOyCx4SpINzffiu2r_4oXakGKzVyqsm7ltUSl2aD0DCkqdEUxppsziWaXMbNrvRioaIWCKYHR8T5EvapuRzazuw9fK4OnTAtz5w8aQNiNNh7FdgykyjcWiKXzymjmpaLJ--xNsUQRVmF0Ku3TrUAFdP3pqGb9ZjE8Jewy3jDYwt0_OfwVLP8NXSgyMej_d9CSeoY4LvCymn-mlaqSDUCroqbNv6bFo2A2K62xiBIJyap4y0V_FtXLTxSC3a4Vd-WP6hx5tcTdSk5PCGnJ11Wk6nTCvx396cRqsncXPMbdynIUy8HGpYDL2x5t4q0t8GhoyuNuwXjCvgQhf00oTXeHGmIb7b-MxYhBrAyKHEDcz1hlcuIxL71aY1NNUoFAYcv7o9gWEXcfSt2eYaC5VZoP1yzJjX6aCNs_HEpegTf1W364kohQBM_u3SATKI2x32tRl4eckR6iX4CSwvRWLLDauS3gHgA6jUV1f5fLBy2Pa_CO9N_10PNAso_nolblmUc3pnOdMl79UFS_8JF66Hl30C5lFwoqlTAckFRoHzPxPogDWzmI6qgw9OVeC_R-B4KYSFyfCO-8S00iPXugASv-DIH5emuW6Zi85g/4sp/EVxvD-HVT8KkXqeJSLbjnw/h28/h001.rHme1bxBnuzUXK2ThR_tOSpuPiDcKeaOQxy2sEVMXtY">unsubscribe</a></div>
</div>
<div class="issue-story">
<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="https://link.mail.beehiiv.com/ss/c/u001.3a5P_SwQzY5x8USD2q4p0iz1rHpSUtegpVZrJ3So0FNwGHjc_kMRu7pForS6g2ZmHWl9MiN1mkog6JS2gYhCeJZlNWDQpBWo6mhwaPf_O7-sSkYyH_StARBoV8Lwq_1ZKBagHqRKsXF-uNcXFJeCt6Oju7m9zTyKgMdjHL89RB_GTSlrSmTaeZsX1kDYj1Mmww1lK65qQMQIk7ae2iUV8VmAAhBlqJD9abrStCPXR90/4sp/EVxvD-HVT8KkXqeJSLbjnw/h13/h001.91VCfHEVnYsAIYEzV_n6axr8nntgWSp1jTedfQUp7iA">WorkOS Pipes: More Context Makes For Smarter Products</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="workos-pipes-more-context-makes-for-smarter-products-c82d6f"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">Users expect apps and agents to reach the tools they already work in. Every integration is a different OAuth flow, token lifecycle, and weeks of infra before you write product code. WorkOS Pipes handles it in one API call: 100+ pre-built connectors (GitHub, Slack, Salesforce, and more), plus OAuth, token refresh, and credential storage. Call the real provider API with a fresh token, every time.</div>
<div class="issue-story-meta">Pointer &middot; Sponsored &middot; via aiste.ulozaite@gmail.com &middot; <a href="https://link.mail.beehiiv.com/ss/c/u001.PFjHD-ii0vvUj1FcNnOS5qqF3eJEkrxympSzSDDZUB29F4951mhY03iCawzSQ6poZedL3n7H-lMzRTsKnY9bvtDfJYA8pICpvyyNYWT98Xtr9WdPcSpxxaa7Q5dr87aiCZFXsxQxqLiYcZ56a4Ef5p6WLiBwZxYfedrFF_eOOfRHMWcN_V3BWcxy6qycqBUNPEYgd7zZVK972EA-M0gdzRxoBADsrrhMTnQr0tDDFTCLi_CYV6TrK5FCv_bJGEc6hagtM5Fa3NC4mcOAZXVMWjN2iWCkvD9DOJOiHCHLdHGLuMRXh9WEOyCx4SpINzffiu2r_4oXakGKzVyqsm7ltUSl2aD0DCkqdEUxppsziWaXMbNrvRioaIWCKYHR8T5EvapuRzazuw9fK4OnTAtz5w8aQNiNNh7FdgykyjcWiKXzymjmpaLJ--xNsUQRVmF0Ku3TrUAFdP3pqGb9ZjE8Jewy3jDYwt0_OfwVLP8NXSgyMej_d9CSeoY4LvCymn-mlaqSDUCroqbNv6bFo2A2K62xiBIJyap4y0V_FtXLTxSC3a4Vd-WP6hx5tcTdSk5PCGnJ11Wk6nTCvx396cRqsncXPMbdynIUy8HGpYDL2x5t4q0t8GhoyuNuwXjCvgQhf00oTXeHGmIb7b-MxYhBrAyKHEDcz1hlcuIxL71aY1NNUoFAYcv7o9gWEXcfSt2eYaC5VZoP1yzJjX6aCNs_HEpegTf1W364kohQBM_u3SATKI2x32tRl4eckR6iX4CSwvRWLLDauS3gHgA6jUV1f5fLBy2Pa_CO9N_10PNAso_nolblmUc3pnOdMl79UFS_8JF66Hl30C5lFwoqlTAckFRoHzPxPogDWzmI6qgw9OVeC_R-B4KYSFyfCO-8S00iPXugASv-DIH5emuW6Zi85g/4sp/EVxvD-HVT8KkXqeJSLbjnw/h28/h001.rHme1bxBnuzUXK2ThR_tOSpuPiDcKeaOQxy2sEVMXtY">unsubscribe</a></div>

<div class="issue-story">
<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="https://link.mail.beehiiv.com/ss/c/u001.Ira8ofUIq_P_lCLURPEgQ8Dok6h2-kZwEtf4UdHH35hxJ6aFiH5pvszk7ynDoO_DzJ21uo-5oofJ2VmhQ8cXb2MIs5zKsMiV2Ye3slnTAhntQusCwLVC9WKp8hT_DG0mYiXKovx0ntKg3wtJlWPmZw/4sl/h7_ivzkxSm-YefoUD5CPxQ/h3/h001.REMHTc9hqeCoH0eAdBK0Nv7_5UkmOe3zUQtzrPOOR-g">Review The Actual Change, Not The File List</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="review-the-actual-change-not-the-file-list-638d56"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">AI writes more code than ever. Reviewing it shouldn&#39;t mean scrolling forty files in alphabetical order. CodeRabbit Review reorganizes any pull request from a flat file list into a structured, layer-by-layer walkthrough - the logical reading order of the change, not the order your platform happens to sort it. Every range gets its own plain-language summary, with sequence diagrams, state machines, and ERDs generated inline wherever a visual earns its place. (1) Cohorts group related files and chunks so you review one idea at a time. (2) Layers order them so foundational changes - data shapes, contracts - come before the code that depends on them. (3) Code Peek lets you click any variable, function, class or type to see its definition and usages without leaving the tab. (4) Semantic Diff cuts past formatting noise to show what actually changed. From the team that pioneered AI code reviews. 2M reviews every week. 6M repos. 15K customers. Free during early access.</div>

<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="https://link.mail.beehiiv.com/ss/c/u001.TDQMwwTUsDB62J0OSnrlnHbh7ovUCCKd0Fu0mjPXLgozHW1ItZlIrqVsH0alx2dLNw0HFk6qvYHDtxR889tzoXC2CU01xIvMsJ7S8ur5B7Vmek2qRWHAEmCitzBQ-SMhtaGpuxiVIah-xHsNHxr3TQ/4sz/vAaJK0zhTUK-tZAJAgmV1w/h12/h001.6MvVSKO1YVdDcuqOVp98Wb-Auc_Uh6wh82JK-X7hTEA">Pin Claude Code &amp; Copilot Across Your Whole Team</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="pin-claude-code-copilot-across-your-whole-team-e09d2c"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">AI tools move too fast for unpinned local setups. Teammates pull different binary versions and dependency trees, and things break silently. Flox lets you pin your entire AI coding environment, from Claude Code and Copilot to CUDA dependencies. Your assistant knows exactly what&#39;s installed, and every engineer runs the exact same setup.</div>

<h2 class="issue-category-label">Kafka</h2>
<div class="issue-story-grid issue-story-grid-single">
<div class="issue-story">
<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="/2026/08/09/stories/broker-visible-vs-client-local-parallelism-169f80/">Broker-visible vs client-local parallelism</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="broker-visible-vs-client-local-parallelism-169f80"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">Share groups in Kafka are designed to add queue-like behavior to a log, not primarily to parallelize work. To figure out how much parallelism you need, Jack suggests multiplying your message rate by average processing time. If each unit of parallelism maps to a broker-managed consumer, costs grow fast. Keeping parallelism on the client side through virtual threads or async tasks is cheaper and requires far fewer connections.</div>
<div class="issue-story-meta">Programming Digest &middot; via aiste.ulozaite@gmail.com &middot; <a href="https://programmingdigest.net/subscribers/91ee7b33-c28b-4a8e-bab4-e5b8bc4f3f47/unsubscribe">unsubscribe</a></div>
</div>
</div>
</div>
<div class="issue-category-rule"></div>
<div class="issue-index-num">27</div>
<div class="issue-category-body">
<h2 class="issue-category-label">Remote Work</h2>
<div class="issue-story-grid issue-story-grid-single">
<div class="issue-story">
<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="/2026/08/09/stories/work-loudly-058b5d/">Work loudly</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="work-loudly-058b5d"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">Remote work removes the ambient visibility that offices provide for free. To compensate, share work as it happens rather than summarizing it afterward: open issues before you have answers, write pull request descriptions that explain your thinking, and post blockers in public channels. This creates a searchable trail that helps managers advocate for you, lets teammates contribute early, and ensures decisions reach stakeholders before they become surprises.</div>
<div class="issue-story-meta">Programming Digest &middot; via aiste.ulozaite@gmail.com &middot; <a href="https://programmingdigest.net/subscribers/91ee7b33-c28b-4a8e-bab4-e5b8bc4f3f47/unsubscribe">unsubscribe</a></div>
</div>
</div>
</div>
<div class="issue-category-rule"></div>
<div class="issue-index-num">28</div>
<div class="issue-category-body">
<h2 class="issue-category-label">Infrastructure</h2>
<div class="issue-story-grid">
<div class="issue-story">
<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="https://programmingdigest.net/links/22767/91ee7b33-c28b-4a8e-bab4-e5b8bc4f3f47/email">Building service topology at scale</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="building-service-topology-at-scale-3f7e98"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">How Netflix built a real-time service topology system to map connections between services at a very large scale. They describe the system&#39;s architecture, the challenges faced in production, and the solutions used to improve performance, reliability, and scalability. The lessons learned show how careful design, continuous testing, and optimization help build efficient distributed systems.</div>
<div class="issue-story-meta">Programming Digest &middot; via aiste.ulozaite@gmail.com &middot; <a href="https://programmingdigest.net/subscribers/91ee7b33-c28b-4a8e-bab4-e5b8bc4f3f47/unsubscribe">unsubscribe</a></div>
</div>
<div class="issue-story">
<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="https://link.mail.beehiiv.com/ss/c/u001.3a5P_SwQzY5x8USD2q4p0m895QuVtqvkW11dSkgIg8KybxMV8KUPaI66jM3yjJgR3CtT0Kxl1NJ1ptXy3xvy8UgtA5nYHoPnp3VA2ULcLoUt0ahaJJjYFjpm2lHBzgrwB2dLxxAkw-v7CSfp4Aq26Tk8OnX2WSgNVPXty6LrY8FbYMvMstkgBJwen7cCaSLN1_N-LEGJc9eEu_LIYQ2CiA/4sl/h7_ivzkxSm-YefoUD5CPxQ/h13/h001.3hn9rMTXrk0QU-9hVXWK_9aqAeHka6BSTAmU-BVT1pA">Agents Write The Code. Environments Are The Bottleneck.</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="agents-write-the-code-environments-are-the-bottleneck-45878d"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">Coding agents pass unit tests but break things that only show up against real services and real data. Shared staging can&#39;t keep up: agents multiply PR volume and every change queues behind the last. Signadot gives each agent and developer their own environment inside one shared cluster, spanning services, databases and queues. Verified PRs, at scale.</div>
<div class="issue-story-meta">Pointer &middot; Sponsored &middot; via aiste.ulozaite@gmail.com &middot; <a href="https://link.mail.beehiiv.com/ss/c/u001.PFjHD-ii0vvUj1FcNnOS5qqF3eJEkrxympSzSDDZUB29F4951mhY03iCawzSQ6poZedL3n7H-lMzRTsKnY9bvtDfJYA8pICpvyyNYWT98Xtr9WdPcSpxxaa7Q5dr87aixq5vJ-Qw1L7gIyWMpCypEOlfXAJm8SQXIm1s7koyy1uCxC7zXuh07rmNPoUGkVv1gKpPEmpnlpiJFRjhqtOsj5IPIisKSGm6bfnPNBb7Os_uB2VVcdMG9bYc6piswwPej6jIwWj01iywjRr50IXFQSKaxPm4ZrdXmrxqvKM393XlcMsOq6gCMoS0C0j4HA1PvXrlhpQEld-gUUeLw4g22XSEMWMM0rWvQNEIuLnCbhCCm0uCKYqat4jKd3YN4nVHAcMCNQ2Ei2GPykfDHxJYVdco3KJX9l_5_H6GMKBlXDS2VJ9uvj1KdE5hvtRDA9JpsnR5JJq8QDHsBioqn8iUu2PSvhMbiubNTphxednjfeRB90DtJQjS-8NSA6TRu0zRbBERlc9vv94VDRiUhF99FmH7qo2VK1oeV_Jt6u7tbyorAOmxErkh7wKn0Smc_5XQRHXd3rPRWgE2WGFygE5_g37YwMdVHLcl3K8bnt4r-3bJbbgfnzVAsUn1p-3X7DwAKEjwRTq5GEQm0T0BNnZwBHPZ4zVWO8iZmjM0-MrtHIBiUCB-w-1LHsLC-onSDSWLza-QTye7jV6s_6dBH0jyCZEwQJz1v6pog6Ys9INTeYxqRrIb-UZfOKTnt4Emg3iN7_Duxbo-6kxT3fCdcClBFkAOisx45q-pqFhHRn1hyXoEReY0GafKB-yw2hqgKANAVRThb3TLuFB4oTYA4sFOmAH6gey7V58qCPOxWbHvy4HvHJ4MlMksMvE7L0nfn14qm57Kwj8zbo_4PLphSDSiPA/4sl/h7_ivzkxSm-YefoUD5CPxQ/h30/h001.fEtOnaRhwDCITS_nqM_VTSWLrYI5DtFh6HjTL9DQUa0">unsubscribe</a></div>

<div class="issue-category-body">
<h2 class="issue-category-label">Linux</h2>
<div class="issue-story-grid issue-story-grid-single">
<div class="issue-story">
<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="/2026/08/09/stories/htop-explained-168f52/">htop explained</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="htop-explained-168f52"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">Linux tools like htop and top expose a lot of system data, and Pēteris walks through what each number and column actually means. Load average is not simply CPU usage — it counts all running and waiting processes, including those stuck on disk or network. Memory columns like VIRT and RES can be misleading, since virtual memory includes mapped but unused space. Pēteris also covers process states, signals, niceness, scheduling, and what each background service on a fresh Linux server actually does.</div>
<div class="issue-story-meta">Programming Digest &middot; via aiste.ulozaite@gmail.com &middot; <a href="https://programmingdigest.net/subscribers/91ee7b33-c28b-4a8e-bab4-e5b8bc4f3f47/unsubscribe">unsubscribe</a></div>
</div>
</div>
</div>
<div class="issue-category-rule"></div>
<div class="issue-index-num">30</div>
<div class="issue-category-body">
<h2 class="issue-category-label">Protocols</h2>
<div class="issue-story-grid issue-story-grid-single">
<div class="issue-story">
<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="/2026/08/09/stories/how-to-create-your-own-decentralized-messenger-protocol-ece2b4/">How to create your own decentralized messenger protocol</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="how-to-create-your-own-decentralized-messenger-protocol-ece2b4"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">Michał guides on building a simple decentralized messaging protocol using federation. He covers server discovery, user identities, end-to-end encryption, public key exchange, message delivery, and server authentication. He shows how clients can encrypt messages while servers securely communicate and verify each other. The approach provides a practical foundation for building a simple, decentralized messenger without relying on a central server.</div>
<div class="issue-story-meta">Programming Digest &middot; via aiste.ulozaite@gmail.com &middot; <a href="https://programmingdigest.net/subscribers/91ee7b33-c28b-4a8e-bab4-e5b8bc4f3f47/unsubscribe">unsubscribe</a></div>
</div>
</div>
</div>
<div class="issue-category-rule"></div>
<div class="issue-index-num">31</div>
<div class="issue-category-body">
<h2 class="issue-category-label">Statistics</h2>
<div class="issue-story-grid issue-story-grid-single">
<div class="issue-story">
<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="/2026/08/09/stories/90-of-the-t-distribution-51f44e/">90% of the t distribution</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="90-of-the-t-distribution-51f44e"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">Gosset found that using a normal distribution to calculate confidence intervals gives results that are too narrow, because it treats an estimated standard deviation as exact. He developed correction factors based on sample size to fix this. With just two data points, you can estimate a standard deviation by multiplying the difference between them by 1.3, which helps judge whether a result is truly unusual.</div>
<div class="issue-story-meta">Programming Digest &middot; via aiste.ulozaite@gmail.com &middot; <a href="https://programmingdigest.net/subscribers/91ee7b33-c28b-4a8e-bab4-e5b8bc4f3f47/unsubscribe">unsubscribe</a></div>
</div>
</div>
</div>

<h2 class="issue-category-label">Autonomous Vehicles</h2>
<div class="issue-story-grid issue-story-grid-single">
<div class="issue-story">
<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="/2026/08/09/stories/uber-is-building-an-autonomous-vehicle-empire-and-here-s-eve-2341a4/">Uber is building an autonomous vehicle empire, and here&#39;s every company it&#39;s using to do it</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="uber-is-building-an-autonomous-vehicle-empire-and-here-s-eve-2341a4"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">Uber has partnered with — and in some cases made direct investments in — about 30 autonomous vehicle companies over the past two years. Here&#39;s the list and the latest on the partnerships.</div>
<div class="issue-story-meta">aiste.ulozaite@gmail.com &middot; <a href="https://link.techcrunch.com/oc/6871770a273e032f335072f3rvvu8.g0h/25e02a64">unsubscribe</a></div>

<h2 class="issue-category-label">Mobility</h2>
<div class="issue-story-grid">
<div class="issue-story">
<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="/2026/08/09/stories/rivian-spinoff-also-to-start-delivering-e-bikes-after-months-c6aeeb/">Rivian spinoff Also to start delivering e-bikes after months of delays</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="rivian-spinoff-also-to-start-delivering-e-bikes-after-months-c6aeeb"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">Also has big plans beyond the TM-B. The startup mostly refers to itself as a &quot;vehicle&quot; company and has plans to make four-wheel pedal-assist cargo vehicles for Amazon.</div>
<div class="issue-story-meta">aiste.ulozaite@gmail.com &middot; <a href="https://link.techcrunch.com/oc/6871770a273e032f335072f3rvvu8.g0h/25e02a64">unsubscribe</a></div>
</div>

<div class="issue-story-blurb">Every leader eventually meets the same person. Arms folded in the meeting. The one who says &quot;we tried this before&quot; before you have finished the sentence. The one whose email replies get a little shorter each time the project is mentioned. It is tempting to label this person difficult, negative, or stuck. It is also, usually, the wrong diagnosis.</div>

<div class="issue-story">
<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="/2026/08/09/stories/lucas-laws-of-project-management-bbe728/">Lucas&#39; Laws of Project Management</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="lucas-laws-of-project-management-bbe728"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">A project succeeds when it solves the problem. Compliance with the original specification is merely one possible way to get there. Scope cannot be known before the work begins. It can only become less wrong as the work proceeds. A fixed date requires variable scope. A fixed scope requires a variable date. Pretending both variables are fixed does not make it so.</div>

<div class="issue-story">
<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="https://link.mail.beehiiv.com/ss/c/u001.J3WaaJcD-G_P1A1xsf5CHvZqa6bLJQTQi_78LfVUM4p0iY-TbMOAKEU0YcY2hxtmpvacM27op-U5HwUCWYLh9cDCTtsUajEZWsG61uL5QYrFH9W2_s1OcPq2iMDjMQEJFwNxRVAIBeloQPGsz9MiKA0a2qVEGKMH_xIsgmeXTLLxkl04D_r8DpgOe-9ZRO6XUiGo5AJba9L71wPROMLwdQ/4ss/FlauVPTqTeGKDEKpox9WIg/h8/h001.XHL_xVMP7l6fUTXlzOYVtQiC4GU9TQLv3YIxENMUIqs">The Best Prioritization Is No Prioritization</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="the-best-prioritization-is-no-prioritization-8e5b95"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">Prioritization is a trap. It wastes time, wastes effort, and delivers worse results than just executing faster. Instead of spending your time prioritizing: (1) Spend more time focusing on building faster. (2) Shift everyone into durable teams that don&#39;t have to do cross-business prioritization at all, and manage your &quot;prioritization&quot; by the resourcing decisions of growing, downsizing, or splitting teams.</div>

</div>
<div class="issue-story">
<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="https://link.mail.beehiiv.com/ss/c/u001.TDQMwwTUsDB62J0OSnrlnPGVw6BhDZwvkE_I6DURmwWY01JoJy7Zp8x9t5W50orNDSB4Y1ENwGkv55zbBwfhQKXmn_ZG66x1uPgHDvelSb6TVaRcVnzf4rDADlgI-sxADZD__i0AMsI8_hF5B6pe2VmXfCJ3uj_aU1N0DhhBsgqR5KNeg6_tHqGJU0mHAnwEV1w2nkikPUwrNpbmn2ADuhprZTdS_t9d-JPJEfQaXkr80dC1Hw8pE4hgwg77_Yh7tIiu8PYdN0c9tsYMqiyhyXBqnKfRIXTjv5wSxxilWwU/4ss/FlauVPTqTeGKDEKpox9WIg/h13/h001.D7cMSYe5b6WFmMnOXsPcSz-ECxNX_EETEygQy99GKZg">Why Everything Breaks At 150 People</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="why-everything-breaks-at-150-people-b64422"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">Cerebras&#39;s engineering post on building their internal knowledge base received millions of views. Here&#39;s what they got right, what they missed, and how to build a knowledge base that compounds as your company and swarm of agents grows—from the person who led knowledge management at Stripe and Uber.</div>

<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="/2026/08/09/stories/engineering-management-after-the-cost-of-code-collapsed-445de2/">Engineering Management After The Cost Of Code Collapsed</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="engineering-management-after-the-cost-of-code-collapsed-445de2"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">I still hear and read what I call the &quot;old rules&quot; repeated over and over: a director should not spend time coding, good work takes time, protect the team from the business, get consensus before you commit, etc... Karim started checking each rule against the assumption underneath it after the introduction of LLMs in his org.</div>

<div class="issue-story-blurb">(1) What does this person know? What&#39;s new for them? (2) Why are we doing this? (3) What do they need in order to do this task? (4) What does great look like? (5) What&#39;s the timeline and priority level? (6) What&#39;s most likely to go wrong? What can I do to prevent this from happening?</div>

<div class="issue-story">
<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="/2026/08/09/stories/the-big-management-lie-overpromising-499aa9/">The Big Management Lie: Overpromising</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="the-big-management-lie-overpromising-499aa9"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">When managers are under pressure, and especially when they&#39;re under pressure to retain a high performer, overpromising is one of the most common tools to reach for. The industry is rife with promises that someone will get a promotion, a new role, a large bonus, 5 more days of vacation, or any number of goodies, large or small.</div>

<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="https://link.mail.beehiiv.com/ss/c/u001.3a5P_SwQzY5x8USD2q4p0hReQ4bRLzOpdwNX2-v3D3oQs-itoK73LDeSzuX1FESjIkfwbosGPEK8TCXaNljOxJaYOFkOiDAj9NfX58T8gOt-px7UFJdnCK03k9mpBPbAWrCaA5q_OysmVefUqiIUjTha3uDLmP3a8gMwwZ4NT1M/4sz/vAaJK0zhTUK-tZAJAgmV1w/h10/h001.rSKPJE54e_zateoDYopnuYkL2Jykp-9zpXJm4gvzvyk">Yes You Can Measure Engineering</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="yes-you-can-measure-engineering-a3377a"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">I&#39;d like to share my favorite way of measuring the value delivery of engineering organizations. It&#39;s something I&#39;ve used at a lot of companies. I call it the ValueSum metric.</div>

<div class="issue-category-body">
<h2 class="issue-category-label">CareerGrowth</h2>
<div class="issue-story-grid">
<div class="issue-story">
<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="/2026/08/09/stories/in-defense-of-not-understanding-your-codebase-6d5614/">In Defense Of Not Understanding Your Codebase</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="in-defense-of-not-understanding-your-codebase-6d5614"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">These are two largely different ways of programming with different methods, practices and cultures. However, the first group is over-represented in online discussion about software engineering. I want to defend the second group against the first. In many software engineering environments, there&#39;s nothing wrong with being in a state of partial understanding. In fact, in large systems a partial understanding is the best you can do.</div>
<div class="issue-story-meta">Pointer &middot; via aiste.ulozaite@gmail.com &middot; <a href="https://link.mail.beehiiv.com/ss/c/u001.PFjHD-ii0vvUj1FcNnOS5qqF3eJEkrxympSzSDDZUB29F4951mhY03iCawzSQ6poZedL3n7H-lMzRTsKnY9bvtDfJYA8pICpvyyNYWT98Xtr9WdPcSpxxaa7Q5dr87ainQ-RSlQwVDc7iNPZ0PIolJl3vfO_4HnCYm31-TN_zlicTxztDdeC8yJbslgJ9gfJT1DFHWSqwwC0jS9YRgJ2xmelP9VbJhslvt3pPQ1c8jB9BiVXc1RRcC9rnVGij1H9RG_t4iS6pguOj7NnKJaw68kno1jIVDfKX5Xd7s0EQpJh59OGrxDDRbiZyEmgGeHnFK_Shx_5Sn-7_ebqtsSg4RuuSzaxoxNapkJYm29qu8TLSsYqkUh2nbeexZdY_stgAa-xZ47HY9m9QTgUJEF3UELFxqqjMwF6YWE5e7Lo2jVdEEvBPprfuFN-ljkWimFvSYw6YR55LYig7V69firkeqPvrzs4btQVziKbsIMSXWdN2q2NnHrtU9Mu9OsFifYAwGJpbs6GuTDi2Llsu8w3pgb5-juXUNpuNs2ScMSxN8ztwt6pN2DiUB5dU7Ic9NotecMM21vK5j3pvvgHp0BU-JqN79AJ4BE0kbEkoUnOHLrpbTODwUtScJmMPCYil7Db2caKnRrvyPSlF9tETgyJHY1KdYKAn2XK_pgyrOxzHGwqRPT3uG31wXftmz6yJ_e6KmREojsNUBfMjvNvgyyC0wAiTBHClafrQERPbSjSaEAREU1VQ6qPKIXYQYziA9gBzMWBF5z8zX9AAIyDbGHgSHbSxW3IgCla-V_VaZuBwihH2Tybpx8t2nw03r7oSXFI1BTCN5I_Op_-X4SsodB68L_38dL1MGp3xnpl5fYbYah16d0G3qv6Cqg0xKQw_e4tDyEHShLtYr47lEA7YXhdOw/4si/c3Fv450_TjO6jsHLxm7gVg/h28/h001.Kzlwg9HytxtmNBSlK0VUDkS1uyOBa6jUH3u8JnLhUgU">unsubscribe</a></div>
</div>
<div class="issue-story">
<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="https://link.mail.beehiiv.com/ss/c/u001.T-cngy-HiLjOztVyxmCjHAiI2yPEX7vUHq-Jru9d4NuIS4jc7RuS31-h8F94faCB2_oeRiAcYGb4BMA6zXeoaJXh-dB_tBvS7iOLIX6f8X-QcmSdxnT_zem_2GNpPAcIoX0UkMGJwZiI_6WkfWlkbQXpjV5ny3vRD4ODUEonbuP8Gm3yc8szRjk61IdDXW8i/4si/c3Fv450_TjO6jsHLxm7gVg/h13/h001.4N6_q6E2okjtcWXQMfQFUnAUx8KvZtUzVLynIS1WVkQ">Don&#39;t Ask What You Want. Ask Who You Want To Be</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="don-t-ask-what-you-want-ask-who-you-want-to-be-82d82d"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">In almost any large dataset drawn from the real world — populations of cities, lengths of rivers, prices, earthquake depths, company revenues — the leading digit is not uniformly distributed. The digit 1 appears as the first digit about 30% of the time. Not 11%. Nearly three times the naive expectation. The digit 9 appears less than 5% of the time.</div>

<h2 class="issue-category-label">CaseStudy</h2>
<div class="issue-story-grid issue-story-grid-single">
<div class="issue-story">
<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="https://link.mail.beehiiv.com/ss/c/u001.3a5P_SwQzY5x8USD2q4p0m895QuVtqvkW11dSkgIg8I7yY6GUJWSXHFbDSHBQ6GNQrjjAo0cEFcRihee9TwhCcKFwXp9kWqArYw-1vKhLC5-SsA8V6KgMm9AiCiXv5o_kXXftqId81GejAQnlxNvwOcWG_rx-MjxBAo7PE2xxKO_ezUQY_eGiIH32EH0LmCK8EP3dG_u-u42b4Cav2j51nnfyb7SgZRujmMPhAdlwfc/4si/c3Fv450_TjO6jsHLxm7gVg/h12/h001.kYlinAwQZCOKQjf6mAXxz0oGvVx8vs3vLmTjRzumNeA">How Bitso Cut Change Failure Rate 83% While Scaling Delivery With Coding Agents</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="how-bitso-cut-change-failure-rate-83-while-scaling-delivery--abb169"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">Bitso&#39;s 250+ engineers and their coding agents push changes across 200+ microservices, and shared staging was always broken. They replaced full-stack preview environments with Signadot sandboxes that fork only the changed services per PR. The result: 83% lower change failure rate, 2.1x deploy frequency, and open-to-deploy down from 12.1 days to 4.6 days.</div>

<h2 class="issue-category-label">DeepDive</h2>
<div class="issue-story-grid">
<div class="issue-story">
<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="https://link.mail.beehiiv.com/ss/c/u001.J3WaaJcD-G_P1A1xsf5CHpaRz8t_03O-U4BCRI-69w35vmwThpdixy-4m-Ruy-nKsvqGsoF_Y2cFk5gCwjg5W1M1kX5Zfl0ZIyxNvEn6NYttk1Kfa0Nz2-8a2RdEqVch056EW7JN9VqV9s4QfwAKPnTIkppgBehbYLG7Djrf62DbwAV476R8at1-o_VPziyHwXl61MRShXPXH2wUbQC2nw/4si/c3Fv450_TjO6jsHLxm7gVg/h14/h001.WWhtamRVmsiEYmkQnL4jEIQoOzUnCybWSRISTnr2Q">Beyond Happy Path Engineering: Time</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="beyond-happy-path-engineering-time-3dc038"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">This series is about making your programs more resilient. Each post looks at one part of the system we take for granted: networks, time, databases, storage, users, dependencies, deployments, queues, and concurrency. The question is always the same: what assumptions are we making here, and what should we do when they stop being true?</div>

<div class="issue-story">
<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="https://link.mail.beehiiv.com/ss/c/u001.yC9eJs5Q3flddlsql05gYTvJwBJ-vkwlVdgjPVnhE5l-nizNZG8hPgWKGPxVTybkAhPGZC2GoXHEc83-HNmlOzAcsWDAOt_DJY9eQiz2GWDXg-LkYw1LOGDDn3DItNuc9UfXr3wqxX6FncOV7UGq8GpJliOaJ25BdX23VXoZxAeMWSDwBsppyHISzdQr6fcl9W9pxqvmOnrihSUokBtBXg/4si/c3Fv450_TjO6jsHLxm7gVg/h15/h001.vf-16H4EMSSdTZDq5RisZPqnDbg-_6aw9HUdd93puJw">The Depth-First Search Pattern: Exploring Trees And Graphs</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="the-depth-first-search-pattern-exploring-trees-and-graphs-23a86d"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">DFS is the pattern you reach for when you need to find a path, check if something is reachable, or explore all possible paths through a structure. It works on trees, graphs, and many problems that can be represented as graphs, even if they don&#39;t look like one at first glance.</div>

<h2 class="issue-category-label">Git</h2>
<div class="issue-story-grid">
<div class="issue-story">
<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="https://link.mail.beehiiv.com/ss/c/u001.WjplEBHoYsVAvmS39jW9CSYIgdBsYm8mMIVCXIjnaRkDmlRbT8bKnR0anQGIxRgyDB_dHO-OZlka26X91OSeA3Z9jZhtGLo1wYYgtfrOab6a5zzrnDnUEICjw4lWvW0SahLLaVlE4hmEnSpOHWVZgl1UPnSYO44Xog9yX_iXJFs/4sp/EVxvD-HVT8KkXqeJSLbjnw/h16/h001.JkHgnPcCKpi-7GDCeTkziYoKol5l6ERdTjVYyXhoHv8">The Git History Command Deserves More Attention</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="the-git-history-command-deserves-more-attention-a9e434"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">Working with lots of changes in parallel on git can be painful. You end up juggling branches and commits, and running scary rebase -i commands that can leave your tree in a half-broken state if you so much as sneeze.</div>

</div>
<div class="issue-story">
<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="https://link.mail.beehiiv.com/ss/c/u001.71LCQkoHczeoiEaexlZjtikp-LYAe0nYTPl4jnMxfDmVDG4FIxDj0DOZHluVl2Ndr0LC0tx2h4aYBXY7gViXb1-mTl9AiZOoHIN8ympScFh1ghDMEjmc3pAWuXpy6hTgVGP875sawlHMWwpyzKTujxBk5kJ1imhH0fT18l32x8c/4sl/h7_ivzkxSm-YefoUD5CPxQ/h16/h001.d5Ow4ZgE9EgKosFPvnSQlkeOdbK-MC8B5OOKad9NMUU">How Big Is A Git Commit?</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="how-big-is-a-git-commit-8e6944"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">It depends. Git stores &quot;objects&quot; as zlib compressed data. So the exact size of a commit will depend on the compressibility of your files (which is basically a function of the amount of repetition in them).</div>

<div class="issue-category-rule"></div>
<div class="issue-index-num">46</div>
<div class="issue-category-body">
<h2 class="issue-category-label">DevEx</h2>
<div class="issue-story-grid issue-story-grid-single">
<div class="issue-story">
<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="/2026/08/09/stories/good-tools-are-invisible-2df7c6/">Good Tools Are Invisible</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="good-tools-are-invisible-2df7c6"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">A good tool is and ought to be invisible - striving to make such tools is the goal of a toolmaker. One habit I see a lot, and have to push back on, is taking a tool&#39;s shortcomings and reselling them as a &quot;puzzle game&quot; which is &quot;fun&quot; to solve. I don&#39;t want my tools to be &quot;fun&quot;. I want my tools to be invisible.</div>

<h2 class="issue-category-label">Communication</h2>
<div class="issue-story-grid issue-story-grid-single">
<div class="issue-story">
<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="https://link.mail.beehiiv.com/ss/c/u001.J3WaaJcD-G_P1A1xsf5CHru0IIAOyixDRQv42w2ooxBnjYlNPBLYocq9kftM-iBLfdd0Dyt1eWNrClZa4vIpKRc-eFNYP9IxCqmWTf3mfrg0Efk4K3f-p3gqScDnHr5mlmnctEDWiLHXO9ybnuFZ4p2zNugh8JNe33aWTKGpyRo/4sw/eBYeDbHiRy6heWHd_5s8-A/h13/h001.eQMr6OKZ21ENb8jIschh65lIf7XnoYcjx5nTsdFcyfo">Work Loudly</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="work-loudly-783cb6"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">In remote work, visibility isn&#39;t ambient anymore. If your work only shows up at the end - or only in your head and DMs—you make it harder for people to help, harder for managers to advocate for you, and easier for the org to miss your impact. Ben shares his approach to working loudly.</div>
<div class="issue-story-meta">Pointer &middot; via aiste.ulozaite@gmail.com &middot; <a href="https://link.mail.beehiiv.com/ss/c/u001.PFjHD-ii0vvUj1FcNnOS5qqF3eJEkrxympSzSDDZUB29F4951mhY03iCawzSQ6poZedL3n7H-lMzRTsKnY9bvtDfJYA8pICpvyyNYWT98Xtr9WdPcSpxxaa7Q5dr87ai6uFLAnJHPUYNZ_J7BqCFg8SfGCSZ2RvxxQ_YXyeI5APHOvfIwzedOgRs16yUt2Mdrzk65sKhvT5_Vex3H6Myts4hOcs230f8vNEvyALU2s2Ucm-k0Fp3QX-LomohRJxkfIljgcgIFvwagNG6l0IpCyL7p-Ych1YvXBrqhEPGcPIYJrn3pqSHK-xH6bbWgu0qoaOjwjMuF4HnskyBDXRyk6ZszOgGsc4xFF3x7zAU2qZW1_gmKq6gW6JM3BkFDC2YpY1Gkciuxv6tdRpJLuWdgkqsrUCA90T9WeZOlJJyZjyfsdhUnu6k_V9Reiw-afbfU80PQU8vNMwY6E7iUfqgbW9FMP8OQPrPr6CA_piENKg9rr62ub9ohhq1GhSOut4pseBRvORT1QbQ6Zkyy_HWRdIufhwRo5EaSdMES20mLAIKA0d_jVS03L9xx1JkOuZZ4oF4LXXkBbIg_NQ5g1rrMGpvL_5xhbzWjuVRriyG7hXR1HYeAxx34jQD1VrmjmftuqF5uIDp6rk_DUJuthMuHf_uwoUjAoatjhMsscf83wTk7gowxckwSawjFYr6RWINTrZEkNl7FlJhVBguKKwD8exFF6tz3LGmmILdGT7HNsvTaxX1ogD4QI9pXflVT1mDJzAY82HkPgIRK3mYBq1Tmtx5yhaIMxiDrcJTtJf54Vp8cPnWJ2tsoaXnnkSryF6tFrWlSgc6bCJwG1zb3n4R0EwW0cjJrCnw9gCcMZ96IVQZd1tuvy9lKiw76em4NpiHu42ssaqkjFgapFbL7pt-xQ/4sw/eBYeDbHiRy6heWHd_5s8-A/h27/h001.-_QYU8Js0GSLkyAzNSHIzqVN-IFIAjkAN8eQIWNrC5A">unsubscribe</a></div>

<h2 class="issue-category-label">Wellbeing</h2>
<div class="issue-story-grid issue-story-grid-single">
<div class="issue-story">
<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="/2026/08/09/stories/finding-calm-in-life-and-work-d84548/">Finding Calm In Life And Work</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="finding-calm-in-life-and-work-d84548"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">In a meta study involving 177 studies and more than 22,000 people, researchers found that being anxious reliably predicts worse performance on various measures, including working memory, which is the part of your mind that does the processing, the reasoning, and the problem solving.</div>
<div class="issue-story-meta">Pointer &middot; via aiste.ulozaite@gmail.com &middot; <a href="https://link.mail.beehiiv.com/ss/c/u001.PFjHD-ii0vvUj1FcNnOS5qqF3eJEkrxympSzSDDZUB29F4951mhY03iCawzSQ6poZedL3n7H-lMzRTsKnY9bvtDfJYA8pICpvyyNYWT98Xtr9WdPcSpxxaa7Q5dr87aic8aJqv7RXdAVcFZrWbDpOdAofbDU3c99KufBb_Z39HjnRFScjFRBf_pzmguh38K1zbC6_DqAJlvm_8OjYhVqRkjbutmKwnAkyUqgYE3nbL8rrBY3mbprvLYzPGGRgZz_Fy01RloV-KQsP6PtmR0i0mLS4fizY5niH8XhcKO4eKaETdX1n-szchRP66JHSigPGCHDC4n0a5OlEQb4o1ap_K-MzH-zucrJOb5wsJe5D2bVtj99LCyqzgmJlcWm8xAA1i8CQyMNNyKXB-NCCvmeHYfh0f-eiLyMvm1qlPMLpz9DgUuaBaTQ2DRSoV4myGPPJUsoJiemJE9rC4rotPZM-32ubZXCtTQOB5qDfVqtuaG4hRu32Cu756iIMS83BHLr9XDswGJxWuR6ymhPk8aGbuyMusNjj-W97dU0FxKs0CS-nn004EUfxBhlSECedJLUcnFsgtjKcyu92KN4SthZ-g2WLzGO4iYLnsz1HKrpk4ZyXusHjiJSCB8UTQ8jTC1YKDJxrb23Gej5pc2ql-nmRyv_AMd_c04_rjZyHvqxX7AKLVcV6VsOO7e5ymQZLHonVa9LvzsSaan6MlfDti55cv1ydHLSY72WIv7v6gml8nfOqIp0j7XFAYnyOPwflAfG7z7VxdO_-a8QcAgLdGz7D8gShbeZElBr8lpem8JzwH-oAEoll_IjgGq2DVMkcAGkQz7NTL4MYUHhitIFm-zCseM2Bvwp7ryZhU1_H-W6A8bYvhYx96v5MG7R1CQ6STjp0942rv__0hpCQ1NvAXroSA/4sz/vAaJK0zhTUK-tZAJAgmV1w/h27/h001.dLif-S5OvzIpE70O9_I-VfhvqNwPvpFhClCl80ncNhA">unsubscribe</a></div>

<h2 class="issue-category-label">Code Quality</h2>
<div class="issue-story-grid issue-story-grid-single">
<div class="issue-story">
<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="https://link.mail.beehiiv.com/ss/c/u001.J3WaaJcD-G_P1A1xsf5CHumQeGqRCN7RkCF-xxyGrUrwYjwWF1PgdRw4ic-Cs3bmh07tyL9ww2wGMnI2Bm5fCtOzlHvC5WX8ZpxFhUBSRcMCZNHmdKw5K9EKj9F-iUhETMg5osrtXwvYDdK2_e8W2-XWqbV0csUOGY1gMow_dcW7n2i235VE1dLzYic6vat37pmvZic4D9Rd_GcbwmjRcGP2mhZ4VSRdKT8jbuozuJI/4sz/vAaJK0zhTUK-tZAJAgmV1w/h13/h001.1ipO1COTP9qmYySSMnUhZ2z7wx5eIFcaNb6E6n6si_I">Beyond &quot;Clean Code&quot;: Why Your Comments Matter</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="beyond-clean-code-why-your-comments-matter-b0781b"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">To the extent that your programming language lets you express these things naturally in your logic, you should do so. But don&#39;t contort your logic to accommodate intent and &quot;why&quot; information that would be easier for your audience to understand in natural language.</div>
<div class="issue-story-meta">Pointer &middot; via aiste.ulozaite@gmail.com &middot; <a href="https://link.mail.beehiiv.com/ss/c/u001.PFjHD-ii0vvUj1FcNnOS5qqF3eJEkrxympSzSDDZUB29F4951mhY03iCawzSQ6poZedL3n7H-lMzRTsKnY9bvtDfJYA8pICpvyyNYWT98Xtr9WdPcSpxxaa7Q5dr87aic8aJqv7RXdAVcFZrWbDpOdAofbDU3c99KufBb_Z39HjnRFScjFRBf_pzmguh38K1zbC6_DqAJlvm_8OjYhVqRkjbutmKwnAkyUqgYE3nbL8rrBY3mbprvLYzPGGRgZz_Fy01RloV-KQsP6PtmR0i0mLS4fizY5niH8XhcKO4eKaETdX1n-szchRP66JHSigPGCHDC4n0a5OlEQb4o1ap_K-MzH-zucrJOb5wsJe5D2bVtj99LCyqzgmJlcWm8xAA1i8CQyMNNyKXB-NCCvmeHYfh0f-eiLyMvm1qlPMLpz9DgUuaBaTQ2DRSoV4myGPPJUsoJiemJE9rC4rotPZM-32ubZXCtTQOB5qDfVqtuaG4hRu32Cu756iIMS83BHLr9XDswGJxWuR6ymhPk8aGbuyMusNjj-W97dU0FxKs0CS-nn004EUfxBhlSECedJLUcnFsgtjKcyu92KN4SthZ-g2WLzGO4iYLnsz1HKrpk4ZyXusHjiJSCB8UTQ8jTC1YKDJxrb23Gej5pc2ql-nmRyv_AMd_c04_rjZyHvqxX7AKLVcV6VsOO7e5ymQZLHonVa9LvzsSaan6MlfDti55cv1ydHLSY72WIv7v6gml8nfOqIp0j7XFAYnyOPwflAfG7z7VxdO_-a8QcAgLdGz7D8gShbeZElBr8lpem8JzwH-oAEoll_IjgGq2DVMkcAGkQz7NTL4MYUHhitIFm-zCseM2Bvwp7ryZhU1_H-W6A8bYvhYx96v5MG7R1CQ6STjp0942rv__0hpCQ1NvAXroSA/4sz/vAaJK0zhTUK-tZAJAgmV1w/h27/h001.dLif-S5OvzIpE70O9_I-VfhvqNwPvpFhClCl80ncNhA">unsubscribe</a></div>

<h2 class="issue-category-label">Documentation</h2>
<div class="issue-story-grid issue-story-grid-single">
<div class="issue-story">
<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="https://link.mail.beehiiv.com/ss/c/u001.71LCQkoHczeoiEaexlZjthwMm64Iym68hKzQdPM2gmuhJAgJbFM9fpwq3g99YEWQjiKAHbDoEQXmvpARi4FxRwwWPxOvdzmKh0Y_rBG7b4rZl3hxUbsxQoE38HcSEKCbLulkNhB99UyWGvaPUaGyx1b-nAom3Phi5Th6siSCE5TazZ49Ntg0-1ClKORgGzaRdf2Q_Go79cxBC2g4AbFUNQ/4sz/vAaJK0zhTUK-tZAJAgmV1w/h15/h001.DLZTsLqjcr9ofA1XAs3PC3RWJQK1djmiStAWkNlkoQI">How To Write An Effective Software Design Document</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="how-to-write-an-effective-software-design-document-5e4710"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">I&#39;ve written design docs as a developer at Google, Microsoft, and within my own companies. The specifics vary, but the underlying principles remain the same. A design doc should articulate the hard problems you&#39;re solving and help your teammates give you feedback. Below, I share my approach to creating effective design docs and explain what belongs in a design doc and what does not.</div>
<div class="issue-story-meta">Pointer &middot; via aiste.ulozaite@gmail.com &middot; <a href="https://link.mail.beehiiv.com/ss/c/u001.PFjHD-ii0vvUj1FcNnOS5qqF3eJEkrxympSzSDDZUB29F4951mhY03iCawzSQ6poZedL3n7H-lMzRTsKnY9bvtDfJYA8pICpvyyNYWT98Xtr9WdPcSpxxaa7Q5dr87aic8aJqv7RXdAVcFZrWbDpOdAofbDU3c99KufBb_Z39HjnRFScjFRBf_pzmguh38K1zbC6_DqAJlvm_8OjYhVqRkjbutmKwnAkyUqgYE3nbL8rrBY3mbprvLYzPGGRgZz_Fy01RloV-KQsP6PtmR0i0mLS4fizY5niH8XhcKO4eKaETdX1n-szchRP66JHSigPGCHDC4n0a5OlEQb4o1ap_K-MzH-zucrJOb5wsJe5D2bVtj99LCyqzgmJlcWm8xAA1i8CQyMNNyKXB-NCCvmeHYfh0f-eiLyMvm1qlPMLpz9DgUuaBaTQ2DRSoV4myGPPJUsoJiemJE9rC4rotPZM-32ubZXCtTQOB5qDfVqtuaG4hRu32Cu756iIMS83BHLr9XDswGJxWuR6ymhPk8aGbuyMusNjj-W97dU0FxKs0CS-nn004EUfxBhlSECedJLUcnFsgtjKcyu92KN4SthZ-g2WLzGO4iYLnsz1HKrpk4ZyXusHjiJSCB8UTQ8jTC1YKDJxrb23Gej5pc2ql-nmRyv_AMd_c04_rjZyHvqxX7AKLVcV6VsOO7e5ymQZLHonVa9LvzsSaan6MlfDti55cv1ydHLSY72WIv7v6gml8nfOqIp0j7XFAYnyOPwflAfG7z7VxdO_-a8QcAgLdGz7D8gShbeZElBr8lpem8JzwH-oAEoll_IjgGq2DVMkcAGkQz7NTL4MYUHhitIFm-zCseM2Bvwp7ryZhU1_H-W6A8bYvhYx96v5MG7R1CQ6STjp0942rv__0hpCQ1NvAXroSA/4sz/vAaJK0zhTUK-tZAJAgmV1w/h27/h001.dLif-S5OvzIpE70O9_I-VfhvqNwPvpFhClCl80ncNhA">unsubscribe</a></div>
